The main weaknesses lie in the potential bias introduced by the judge model and limitations in task family and a manual process for creating the tasks. However, these are acknowledged by the authors.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### HOI-PAGE: Zero-Shot Human-Object Interaction Generation with Part Affordance Guidance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07209v1)
- **Authors**: Lei Li, Angela Dai
- **Abstract**: We present HOI-PAGE, a new approach to synthesizing 4D human-object interactions (HOIs) from text prompts in a zero-shot fashion, driven by part-level affordance reasoning. In contrast to prior works that focus on global, whole body-object motion for 4D HOI synthesis, we observe that generating realistic and diverse HOIs requires a finer-grained understanding -- at the level of how human body parts engage with object parts. We thus introduce Part Affordance Graphs (PAGs), a structured HOI representation distilled from large language models (LLMs) that encodes fine-grained part information along with contact relations. We then use these PAGs to guide a three-stage synthesis: first, decomposing input 3D objects into geometric parts; then, generating reference HOI videos from text prompts, from which we extract part-based motion constraints; finally, optimizing for 4D HOI motion sequences that not only mimic the reference dynamics but also satisfy part-level contact constraints. Extensive experiments show that our approach is flexible and capable of generating complex multi-object or multi-person interaction sequences, with significantly improved realism and text alignment for zero-shot 4D HOI generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the HOI-PAGE paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HOI-PAGE, a novel zero-shot approach for generating 4D human-object interactions (HOIs) from text prompts.  The key innovation lies in the use of Part Affordance Graphs (PAGs), which are distilled from Large Language Models (LLMs) to guide the HOI synthesis process.  PAGs represent fine-grained, part-level affordances between human body parts and object parts, encoding contact relations and motion constraints. The method consists of three stages: (1) object part segmentation, (2) HOI reference video synthesis guided by the PAG, and (3) 4D HOI motion optimization using the PAG to enforce contact and realism. The method demonstrates results on a variety of interaction scenarios, including multi-person and multi-object cases, and shows improved realism and text alignment compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty of the paper stems from the explicit modeling of part-level affordances to guide HOI generation in a zero-shot setting.  While prior work has explored HOI generation and the use of LLMs for visual understanding, the combination of part-level affordance graphs, their distillation from LLMs, and their use as a structured constraint within a comprehensive HOI synthesis pipeline represents a significant advance. It allows handling more complex interactions not easily covered by the previous interaction paradigms.  The decomposition into the three stages, each leveraging the PAG, also demonstrates a well-structured and thoughtful design.

*   **Significance:** The significance of HOI-PAGE lies in its ability to generate realistic and diverse HOI motions without requiring expensive and limited 4D interaction datasets for training. This has several implications:

    *   **Generalization:** It allows the synthesis of interactions with novel objects and scenarios that were previously inaccessible.
    *   **Flexibility:** The PAG-guided approach enables modeling of complex interactions involving multiple people and objects, which existing methods often struggle with.
    *   **Accessibility:**  By removing the need for large datasets, HOI-PAGE makes HOI synthesis more accessible to researchers and practitioners.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong results:**  Qualitative and quantitative results, including perceptual studies, demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach in generating more realistic and text-aligned HOIs.
    *   **Comprehensive approach:** The method addresses multiple aspects of HOI generation, including object segmentation, motion synthesis, and optimization.
    *   **Clear presentation:**  The paper is well-written and clearly explains the method and its components. The supplementary material is also well organized, and the analysis comprehensive.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on LLMs:** The quality of the PAGs depends on the reasoning capabilities of the underlying LLM. Errors or biases in the LLM could propagate through the synthesis pipeline. Further, there is a black-box character to this process which limits the ability to tune or interpret the generated PAGs.

    *   **Computational Cost:**  The optimization process and the use of video diffusion models can be computationally expensive. This could limit its applicability to real-time applications.

    *   **Lack of fine-grained control:** While the PAGs provide part-level guidance, capturing detailed motions beyond the part level (e.g., individual finger articulations) remains a challenge.
*   **Potential Impact:** HOI-PAGE has the potential to significantly impact several fields, including:

    *   **Character animation:**  Generating realistic character animations interacting with objects.
    *   **Virtual reality/Augmented reality (VR/AR):** Creating immersive virtual environments with believable interactions.
    *   **Robotics:** Training robots to perform complex tasks involving object manipulation.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:**  While HOI-PAGE addresses an important problem with a well-designed solution and delivers promising results, some of the steps still depend on expensive and less-controllable large language models. Furthermore, the limitations regarding extremely nuanced and delicate motions mean that the interactions, whilst better and more semantically relevant than SoTA, are not fully resolved. However, the zero-shot nature of this approach, its ability to generalize to novel objects, and the explicit modeling of part affordances makes it a strong contender, and highly relevant for these new challenges.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Sword and Shield: Uses and Strategies of LLMs in Navigating Disinformation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07211v1)
- **Authors**: Gionnieve Lim, Bryan Chen Zhengyu Tan, Kellie Yu Hui Sim, Weiyan Shi, Ming Hui Chew, Ming Shan Hee, Roy Ka-Wei Lee, Simon T. Perrault, Kenny Tsu Wei Choo
- **Abstract**: The emergence of Large Language Models (LLMs) presents a dual challenge in the fight against disinformation. These powerful tools, capable of generating human-like text at scale, can be weaponised to produce sophisticated and persuasive disinformation, yet they also hold promise for enhancing detection and mitigation strategies. This paper investigates the complex dynamics between LLMs and disinformation through a communication game that simulates online forums, inspired by the game Werewolf, with 25 participants. We analyse how Disinformers, Moderators, and Users leverage LLMs to advance their goals, revealing both the potential for misuse and combating disinformation. Our findings highlight the varying uses of LLMs depending on the participants' roles and strategies, underscoring the importance of understanding their effectiveness in this context. We conclude by discussing implications for future LLM development and online platform design, advocating for a balanced approach that empowers users and fosters trust while mitigating the risks of LLM-assisted disinformation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the interplay between Large Language Models (LLMs) and disinformation within online forums. It employs a communication game, inspired by Werewolf, where participants assume roles of Disinformers, Moderators, and Users, all equipped with LLMs. The study analyzes how these actors leverage LLMs to either spread or combat disinformation. The findings highlight the potential misuse of LLMs in generating persuasive disinformation, but also their utility in enhancing detection and mitigation strategies.  The paper underscores the significance of understanding the varying effectiveness of LLMs depending on the user's role and strategy and advocates for a balanced approach in developing LLMs and designing online platforms to mitigate the risks of LLM-assisted disinformation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its methodology, directly observing disinformation strategies in a controlled, yet dynamic, simulated environment. While studies have explored LLMs in disinformation, this paper distinguishes itself by focusing on the strategic interactions among different actors and their reliance on LLMs. It's particularly valuable that the authors directly observe how disinformers operate and adapt when provided with LLMs. Most work relies on more indirect methods of inference.

**Significance:** The paper's significance is multi-faceted. First, it provides empirical evidence of the dual-use nature of LLMs in the context of online disinformation. Second, it identifies key factors influencing the effectiveness of LLMs, such as social cohesion, disruption to group norms, and level of critical engagement. Third, it generates valuable insights for future LLM development and online platform design. The observations that unpopular opinions draw suspicion, but inconspicous and amiable behavior evade suspicion, and the LLMs are more effective in groups that are engaged and critical of each other are important. The recommendations for increasing the effectiveness of tools to limit disinformers are also strong.

**Strengths:**
*   **Methodological Rigor:** The use of a communication game provides a controlled environment for studying complex social dynamics.  The think-aloud protocols, observations, and post-game interviews offer rich qualitative data.
*   **Empirical Findings:**  The paper delivers actionable insights on how LLMs are being used (and potentially misused) in online environments. The study identifies specific strategies employed by different actors (Disinformers, Moderators, Users) and reveals their relative effectiveness.
*   **Balanced Perspective:** The authors avoid a purely alarmist or utopian view of LLMs, recognizing both their potential for harm and for good. The paper advocates for a balanced approach, emphasizing the importance of human oversight and critical engagement.

**Weaknesses:**
*   **Limited Scale and Generalizability:** The study involved a relatively small sample size (25 participants), and the findings may not fully generalize to larger, more diverse online communities.  Moreover, the Werewolf game, while providing a useful framework, is a simplified representation of real-world online interactions. The authors do aknowledge this limitation.
*   **Potential for Artificiality:** Participants were aware of their roles in the communication game, potentially influencing their behavior and responses.  The controlled environment may not fully capture the nuances of real-world disinformation campaigns. Again, the authors do aknowledge this.
*   **LLM Model Choice:** The choice of Nous-Hermes-2-Mixtral-8x7B-DPO, while state-of-the-art at the time, raises concerns about the representativeness of the results, given that the landscape of LLMs changes quickly and that model is far less powerful than modern models like GPT-4. However, focusing on a slightly less powerful model may have allowed the disinformers in the game more control over what disinformaion the model put out.

**Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of LLM-based tools for online platforms, informing the development of features that promote critical engagement, transparency, and user empowerment.  It can also contribute to policy discussions regarding the regulation of LLMs in the context of online disinformation.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of 7. The paper is a well-executed study that makes a meaningful contribution to the field.  The strengths of its methodology and empirical findings outweigh the limitations of its scale and generalizability. The identification of obstacles to the effective use of LLMs is insightful, and the recommendations for future research and policy are practical. While other studies have explored disinformation and LLMs, few have adopted the method of simulating a game to analyze how actors operate with them. Further research could explore different settings in the game, or test new strategies.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### BIMgent: Towards Autonomous Building Modeling via Computer-use Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07217v1)
- **Authors**: Zihan Deng, Changyu Du, Stavros Nousias, André Borrmann
- **Abstract**: Existing computer-use agents primarily focus on general-purpose desktop automation tasks, with limited exploration of their application in highly specialized domains. In particular, the 3D building modeling process in the Architecture, Engineering, and Construction (AEC) sector involves open-ended design tasks and complex interaction patterns within Building Information Modeling (BIM) authoring software, which has yet to be thoroughly addressed by current studies. In this paper, we propose BIMgent, an agentic framework powered by multimodal large language models (LLMs), designed to enable autonomous building model authoring via graphical user interface (GUI) operations. BIMgent automates the architectural building modeling process, including multimodal input for conceptual design, planning of software-specific workflows, and efficient execution of the authoring GUI actions. We evaluate BIMgent on real-world building modeling tasks, including both text-based conceptual design generation and reconstruction from existing building design. The design quality achieved by BIMgent was found to be reasonable. Its operations achieved a 32% success rate, whereas all baseline models failed to complete the tasks (0% success rate). Results demonstrate that BIMgent effectively reduces manual workload while preserving design intent, highlighting its potential for practical deployment in real-world architectural modeling scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and rigorous evaluation of the paper "BIMgent: Towards Autonomous Building Modeling via Computer-use Agents":

**Summary**

The paper introduces BIMgent, an agentic framework that utilizes multimodal large language models (LLMs) to automate architectural building modeling via GUI operations within BIM authoring software. BIMgent handles multimodal inputs like text descriptions and 2D floorplan sketches, plans software-specific workflows, and executes GUI actions. It features a hierarchical planning structure (high-level and low-level planners), distinct action workflows (Pure-Action and Vision-Driven), and a dynamic GUI grounding method to reduce visual noise. The framework incorporates real-time feedback and self-reflection through LLM-based judges to correct mistakes.  The paper evaluates BIMgent using a custom Mini Building Benchmark comprising 25 real-world modeling tasks, demonstrating a 32% success rate compared to baseline models' 0% success rate.  The authors argue BIMgent effectively reduces manual workload while preserving design intent, offering potential for practical deployment.

**Critical Evaluation**

**Novelty:**

*   **Incremental, but Relevant:** The core concept of using computer-use agents for software automation is not entirely new. However, its application to the highly specialized domain of BIM authoring and the AEC sector presents a novel contribution. Existing works primarily focus on web, mobile, or office software, whereas the intricacies and complexities of BIM software (complex GUIs, open-ended design tasks, a large number of operations and complex visual grounding) have been largely underexplored.
*   **Hierarchical Planning & GUI Grounding:** The proposed hierarchical planning structure and the dynamic GUI grounding mechanism demonstrate a significant departure from existing computer-use agent designs, and are geared towards coping with complexity and open-endedness that characterize building design. The hierarchical planning tackles a complex search space and task decomposition while the GUI grounding enhances robustness and reduces visual noise, which are critical for BIM software.
*   **Integration & Evaluation:** The paper introduces an integration of multiple LLMs, vector databases, a RAG approach for software documentation, and specifically addresses both design intent as well as GUI operations. The authors also introduce a dedicated benchmark, addressing a crucial gap in the existing research, where standardized benchmarks for such tasks are lacking.
*   **Limited theoretical contributions:** Despite the engineering innovation, the theoretical novelty is somewhat limited. The contributions are primarily practical implementations rather than significant advancements in LLM architectures, learning algorithms, or fundamental AI principles.

**Significance:**

*   **Potential Impact:** Automating BIM modeling tasks can significantly reduce the manual effort and time consumption for architects and engineers. This would allow them to focus on higher-level design decisions and innovation.
*   **Practical Applicability:** BIMgent demonstrates a promising step toward the development of more accessible and user-friendly BIM tools. The ability to interpret design intent from various inputs (text, sketches) and automatically generate 3D models could democratize the design process.
*   **Addresses a Real-World Problem:** The AEC sector is characterized by increasing complexity and demanding projects. AI-driven automation can help streamline workflows, reduce errors, and improve overall efficiency.
*   **Scope for Future Research:** The work provides a solid foundation for future research in this area. Further improvements in accuracy, efficiency, and generalization across different BIM software platforms are possible. The architecture itself can serve as a template for specialized tasks.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Framework:** The BIMgent framework covers the entire building modeling process, from design interpretation to 3D model generation.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results on the Mini Building Benchmark demonstrate a significant improvement over baseline models.
*   **Detailed Ablation Study:** The ablation study provides valuable insights into the contribution of each component of the framework.
*   **Addresses a Significant Gap:**  It tackles a specific yet important problem in a vital but underserved domain.
*   **Well-defined Evaluation:** The evaluation methodology, including design and operation evaluations, is clear and rigorous.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Generalization:** The evaluation is limited to Vectorworks. Further research is required to assess the framework's generalizability to other BIM authoring software.
*   **Error Analysis:** The error analysis, while helpful, could be more in-depth to identify specific areas for improvement.
*   **Benchmark Size:** The Mini Building Benchmark is a good start, but a larger and more diverse dataset would be beneficial.
*   **Reliance on Existing LLMs:** The framework relies heavily on the capabilities of existing LLMs.  Improvements in LLM performance will directly translate to improvements in BIMgent.
*   **Scalability:**  The computational cost and the speed of the process need more discussion.  Automating tasks is useless if it consumes far too much time and compute.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7.5** to this paper.

*   The core idea of applying LLMs in the described way is logical but not incredibly ingenious. There exist prior applications in game environments, however this work offers specialization and the described modular architecture, hierarchical planning, pure and vision action workflows, etc..
*   The work offers a well-designed, functional framework that addresses the complexity of BIM authoring, but it relies on existing LLMs and architectures.
*   The significance lies in the potential to automate tedious tasks and democratize architectural modeling, however, the true value depends on scalability, the potential for improvements, and its widespread adoption.
*   The solid experimental results and the insightful ablation study strengthen the paper's contribution. The Mini Building Benchmark is a valuable addition to the field, even though it requires expansion.

The paper's strengths lie in its well-engineered implementation, strong empirical results, and its focus on a practical, real-world problem. While the theoretical novelty may be limited, the framework's potential impact on the AEC sector and its contribution to the development of more accessible BIM tools warrant a favorable assessment.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Advancing Multimodal Reasoning Capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models via Visual Perception Reward
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07218v1)
- **Authors**: Tong Xiao, Xin Xu, Zhenya Huang, Hongyu Gao, Quan Liu, Qi Liu, Enhong Chen
- **Abstract**: Enhancing the multimodal reasoning capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) is a challenging task that has attracted increasing attention in the community. Recently, several studies have applied Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) to the multimodal domain in order to enhance the reasoning abilities of MLLMs. However, these works largely overlook the enhancement of multimodal perception capabilities in MLLMs, which serve as a core prerequisite and foundational component of complex multimodal reasoning. Through McNemar's test, we find that existing RLVR method fails to effectively enhance the multimodal perception capabilities of MLLMs, thereby limiting their further improvement in multimodal reasoning. To address this limitation, we propose Perception-R1, which introduces a novel visual perception reward that explicitly encourages MLLMs to perceive the visual content accurately, thereby can effectively incentivizing both their multimodal perception and reasoning capabilities. Specifically, we first collect textual visual annotations from the CoT trajectories of multimodal problems, which will serve as visual references for reward assignment. During RLVR training, we employ a judging LLM to assess the consistency between the visual annotations and the responses generated by MLLM, and assign the visual perception reward based on these consistency judgments. Extensive experiments on several multimodal reasoning benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of our Perception-R1, which achieves state-of-the-art performance on most benchmarks using only 1,442 training data.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Advancing Multimodal Reasoning Capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models via Visual Perception Reward":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the limitation of existing Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) methods in enhancing the multimodal reasoning capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). The authors argue that current RLVR methods primarily focus on improving reasoning abilities based on answer accuracy, neglecting the crucial role of visual perception. They propose Perception-R1, a novel approach that introduces a visual perception reward to explicitly encourage MLLMs to perceive visual content accurately. This reward is assigned based on the consistency between visual annotations (extracted from CoT trajectories of a proprietary MLLM) and the responses generated by the MLLM during training, as evaluated by a judging LLM. Experiments on several multimodal reasoning benchmarks demonstrate that Perception-R1 achieves state-of-the-art performance using a relatively small training dataset. The key innovation lies in addressing the reward sparsity issue in multimodal perception, leading to more effective training and improved overall reasoning performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the introduction of a specific visual perception reward within the RLVR framework. While RLVR itself is not new, the application of a targeted reward signal to enhance *perception* rather than just reasoning or answer accuracy is a valuable contribution. Extracting visual annotations from CoT trajectories and using a separate LLM as a "judge" is a practical implementation choice, but not inherently novel on its own. The approach to encouraging perceptual accuracy, however, is distinct from works simply scaling datasets and models.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the paper's ability to improve MLLM performance with a relatively small dataset (1442 samples). This suggests the potential for more efficient and cost-effective training of MLLMs, especially in resource-constrained environments. The analysis highlighting the bottleneck of multimodal perception in existing RLVR methods is also significant, providing a clear direction for future research. The demonstrated performance gains on various multimodal benchmarks provide empirical evidence supporting the effectiveness of the proposed approach. Showing state-of-the-art performance with such a small dataset indicates the potential for future work to focus on reward structure to enable more rapid training.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined approach with a clear explanation of the visual perception reward mechanism.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation on several benchmarks.
    *   Demonstrated superior performance compared to existing methods.
    *   Analysis of the impact of different components and judging LLMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Reliance on a proprietary MLLM (Gemini 2.5 Pro) for generating CoT trajectories and Qwen2.5-32B-IT as the judging LLM introduces potential concerns about reproducibility and accessibility. While this may be unavoidable given the current state of the art, it limits the broader impact and verifiability of the results.
    *   The dataset is limited to 1,442 examples. It remains unclear how well the approach would scale to larger and more diverse datasets or generalize to different tasks beyond mathematical reasoning.
    *   The visual annotations are, to some degree, based on *interpretations* of the CoT trajectories rather than pure "facts" about the image.
    *   The improvement on MathVision is not as substantial as on other benchmarks, suggesting possible limitations in the approach. The MathVision benchmark is the largest in terms of number of problems.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of future RLVR methods for MLLMs by emphasizing the importance of visual perception. The proposed visual perception reward mechanism could be adapted and extended to other tasks and modalities. The focus on data efficiency could also inspire research into alternative reward structures and training strategies that reduce the reliance on massive datasets.

*   **Concerns/Missing Information:** The study may have been more impactful if the authors had explored:
    *   Methods for creating visual annotations without proprietary MLLMs.
    *   Evaluated other benchmarks (not just math-related tasks).
    *   The limitations of MLLMs' perceptual capability with other more detailed visual tasks.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by identifying and addressing the limitations of existing RLVR methods for MLLMs. The introduction of the visual perception reward is a novel and effective approach that leads to improved performance and data efficiency. However, the reliance on proprietary LLMs for data generation and judgment limits the broader impact and reproducibility of the results. While the paper demonstrates promising results, further research is needed to validate the approach on larger and more diverse datasets and to explore alternative implementations that do not depend on proprietary technology.  Because of these factors, the score is a strong but not outstanding 7.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### LLM-Enhanced Rapid-Reflex Async-Reflect Embodied Agent for Real-Time Decision-Making in Dynamically Changing Environments
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07223v1)
- **Authors**: Yangqing Zheng, Shunqi Mao, Dingxin Zhang, Weidong Cai
- **Abstract**: In the realm of embodied intelligence, the evolution of large language models (LLMs) has markedly enhanced agent decision making. Consequently, researchers have begun exploring agent performance in dynamically changing high-risk scenarios, i.e., fire, flood, and wind scenarios in the HAZARD benchmark. Under these extreme conditions, the delay in decision making emerges as a crucial yet insufficiently studied issue. We propose a Time Conversion Mechanism (TCM) that translates inference delays in decision-making into equivalent simulation frames, thus aligning cognitive and physical costs under a single FPS-based metric. By extending HAZARD with Respond Latency (RL) and Latency-to-Action Ratio (LAR), we deliver a fully latency-aware evaluation protocol. Moreover, we present the Rapid-Reflex Async-Reflect Agent (RRARA), which couples a lightweight LLM-guided feedback module with a rule-based agent to enable immediate reactive behaviors and asynchronous reflective refinements in situ. Experiments on HAZARD show that RRARA substantially outperforms existing baselines in latency-sensitive scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the issue of inference latency in embodied AI agents operating in dynamic environments, specifically within the HAZARD benchmark. It proposes a Time Conversion Mechanism (TCM) to translate inference delays into equivalent simulation frames, thus accounting for the real-world cost of deliberation.  The paper introduces the Rapid-Reflex Async-Reflect Agent (RRARA), which combines a low-latency rule-based agent for immediate reactions with an asynchronous LLM-based reflector for in-situ action refinement.  Experiments demonstrate RRARA's superior performance compared to existing baselines in latency-sensitive HAZARD scenarios.  The paper also introduces two new metrics: Respond Latency (RL) and Latency-to-Action Ratio (LAR) to quantify agent responsiveness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the combination of several ideas:

*   **TCM (Time Conversion Mechanism):**  While the idea of factoring in computation time into simulated environment performance isn't entirely novel *per se*, its explicit formulation within the context of embodied AI and the HAZARD benchmark, and its integration with the FPS metric is a useful contribution. It provides a more realistic and complete picture of agent performance in dynamic settings.
*   **RRARA (Rapid-Reflex Async-Reflect Agent):** The hybrid architecture, coupling a fast, reactive policy with asynchronous, LLM-driven reflection, is a more significant contribution. The idea of blending reactive and deliberative control is not new, but the specific implementation with an LLM providing asynchronous feedback is well-executed. This design allows for both immediate responses and later refinements based on deeper reasoning, addressing a critical challenge in dynamic environments.
*   **Latency-Aware Metrics (RL and LAR):**  Introducing RL and LAR is a good step towards more comprehensive evaluation. While not groundbreaking on their own, these metrics complement TCM by explicitly quantifying latency and its relationship to action execution.

**Significance:**

*   **Addressing a Real Problem:** The paper tackles a crucial limitation of many embodied AI benchmarks, which often ignore or downplay the impact of inference latency. By focusing on dynamic environments and explicitly modeling computation time, the paper highlights the importance of real-time responsiveness.
*   **Practical Agent Design:** RRARA provides a practical architectural pattern for building more robust agents that can operate effectively in dynamic and unpredictable situations. The modular design allows for swapping in different reactive policies and LLMs depending on the specific task and resource constraints.
*   **Improved Evaluation:** The TCM and the new latency-aware metrics contribute to a more realistic and informative evaluation framework for embodied AI agents. This encourages the development of algorithms that prioritize both accuracy and efficiency.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of inference latency and its impact on agent performance.
*   **Well-Defined Solution:** The proposed TCM and RRARA architecture are well-defined and explained.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The experimental results on the HAZARD benchmark provide evidence of RRARA's effectiveness.
*   **Relevant Metrics:** The introduction of RL and LAR provides a more complete picture of agent performance.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Generalizability of Experiments:** While the HAZARD benchmark is relevant, the specific tasks and environment may not fully capture the complexity of all real-world scenarios. More diverse environments would strengthen the claims.
*   **Rule-Based Reflex:** The rule-based reflex policy is somewhat simplistic and might limit the agent's adaptability in more complex situations.  Exploring more sophisticated reflex policies learned using RL might improve results further.
*   **Limited Analysis of LLM Reflector:** There's not much discussion on the LLM prompting, which could be a key component. How sensitive is performance to different prompts and LLM choices? A more detailed analysis of the LLM's reasoning process and the types of corrections it makes would add value.
*   **Lack of comparison to more complex baselines:** Comparison to offline-RL based methods with lower inference times would improve the analysis.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7**. While the core idea of factoring in inference latency is not completely original, the paper makes a valuable contribution by:

1.  Providing a practical and well-designed agent architecture (RRARA) that addresses this issue in a novel way.
2.  Presenting a concrete mechanism (TCM) and metrics (RL and LAR) for quantifying latency in embodied AI benchmarks.
3.  Validating the approach empirically on a relevant benchmark.

The weaknesses related to limited generalizability and a somewhat simplistic reflex policy prevent a higher score.  However, the paper's clear problem definition, well-defined solution, and improved evaluation methodology make it a significant contribution to the field and likely to influence future research in latency-aware embodied AI.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Hallucination at a Glance: Controlled Visual Edits and Fine-Grained Multimodal Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07227v1)
- **Authors**: Tianyi Bai, Yuxuan Fan, Jiantao Qiu, Fupeng Sun, Jiayi Song, Junlin Han, Zichen Liu, Conghui He, Wentao Zhang, Binhang Yuan
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have achieved strong performance on vision-language tasks but still struggle with fine-grained visual differences, leading to hallucinations or missed semantic shifts. We attribute this to limitations in both training data and learning objectives. To address these issues, we propose a controlled data generation pipeline that produces minimally edited image pairs with semantically aligned captions. Using this pipeline, we construct the Micro Edit Dataset (MED), containing over 50K image-text pairs spanning 11 fine-grained edit categories, including attribute, count, position, and object presence changes. Building on MED, we introduce a supervised fine-tuning (SFT) framework with a feature-level consistency loss that promotes stable visual embeddings under small edits. We evaluate our approach on the Micro Edit Detection benchmark, which includes carefully balanced evaluation pairs designed to test sensitivity to subtle visual variations across the same edit categories. Our method improves difference detection accuracy and reduces hallucinations compared to strong baselines, including GPT-4o. Moreover, it yields consistent gains on standard vision-language tasks such as image captioning and visual question answering. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of combining targeted data and alignment objectives for enhancing fine-grained visual reasoning in MLLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "HALLUCINATION AT A GLANCE: Controlled Visual Editing and Fine-Grained Multimodal Learning":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the issue of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) struggling with fine-grained visual differences, leading to hallucinations and missed semantic shifts. The authors attribute this to limitations in training data and learning objectives.  They propose a controlled data generation pipeline to create minimally edited image pairs with semantically aligned captions, resulting in the Micro Edit Dataset (MED).  They also introduce a supervised fine-tuning (SFT) framework with a feature-level consistency loss to promote stable visual embeddings. They evaluate their approach on a new benchmark, the Micro Edit Detection benchmark, and show improvements in difference detection accuracy and reduced hallucinations compared to strong baselines, including GPT-4o, as well as gains on standard vision-language tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper exhibits good novelty across several dimensions:

    *   **MED Dataset:** The construction of the MED dataset, with its focus on minimal visual edits and aligned captions, fills a significant gap in the availability of training data for MLLMs.  Existing datasets often lack this controlled variability.
    *   **Micro Edit Detection Benchmark:** The careful design of the benchmark, with balanced evaluation pairs testing sensitivity to subtle visual variations, provides a valuable tool for assessing the fine-grained reasoning abilities of MLLMs. The inclusion of both synthetic and real-world samples enhances its robustness.
    *   **Feature Consistency Regularization:** The introduction of a feature consistency regularization term in the SFT framework is a novel approach to encouraging stable visual embeddings under small edits.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a crucial challenge in MLLM research: improving the ability to understand and reason about fine-grained visual details.  This ability is essential for deploying MLLMs in real-world applications that require high precision, such as robotics, industrial quality control, and medical imaging. The demonstrated improvements on both edit detection and standard vision-language tasks suggest that the proposed approach has the potential to significantly enhance the reliability and robustness of MLLMs. The open sourcing of the code and the dataset significantly increases its value to the research community.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined Problem:** The paper clearly identifies and articulates the problem of MLLM brittleness in the face of fine-grained visual changes.
    *   **Comprehensive Approach:** The authors address the problem through both data augmentation and a novel training objective.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results on both the Micro Edit Detection benchmark and standard vision-language tasks provide compelling evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The comparisons to strong baselines, including GPT-4o, further highlight the significance of the improvements.
    *   **Thorough Analysis:**  The ablation study provides insights into the contributions of different components of the proposed framework.
    *   **Generalization:** The real world data augmentation provides some support of generalization.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Synthetic Data Overfitting:** A potential concern is the reliance on synthetic data generated by a controlled editing pipeline. While the inclusion of real-world image pairs in the evaluation mitigates this concern to some extent, further investigation into the generalizability of the approach to more diverse and uncontrolled visual changes would be valuable. It is possible that performance could be artificially inflated by overfit on the biases present in this pipeline.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The framework focuses primarily on binary edits between image pairs. Extending the approach to handle multi-step transformations, temporal reasoning, and compositional edits would broaden its applicability.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The use of full fine-tuning can be computationally expensive. Exploring more efficient adaptation methods would be beneficial.
    *   **Reliance on specific MLLMs:** It will be useful to establish whether other MLLMs can benefit from this approach.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a significant influence on the field of multimodal learning.  The MED dataset and Micro Edit Detection benchmark provide valuable resources for researchers working to improve the fine-grained visual reasoning abilities of MLLMs. The proposed SFT framework with feature consistency regularization offers a promising approach to addressing the challenge of hallucination and improving robustness.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The score is based on the paper's notable novelty in its dataset creation and training objective, its significance in tackling a critical MLLM limitation, its strong empirical results, and its potential to influence future research in the field. While the reliance on synthetic data and limited scope represent weaknesses, the strengths of the paper outweigh these limitations.

### Learn as Individuals, Evolve as a Team: Multi-agent LLMs Adaptation in Embodied Environments
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07232v1)
- **Authors**: Xinran Li, Chenjia Bai, Zijian Li, Jiakun Zheng, Ting Xiao, Jun Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) possess extensive knowledge bases and strong reasoning capabilities, making them promising tools for complex, multi-agent planning in embodied environments. However, despite LLMs' advanced abilities and the sophisticated modular design of agentic methods, existing LLM-based planning algorithms remain limited by weak adaptation capabilities to multi-agent embodied scenarios. We address this limitation by introducing a framework that enables LLM agents to learn and evolve both before and during test time, equipping them with environment-relevant knowledge for better planning and enhanced communication for improved cooperation. Inspired by centralized training with decentralized execution in multi-agent reinforcement learning, we propose a \textit{Learn as Individuals, Evolve as a Team (LIET)} paradigm for multi-agent LLMs adaptation. At the individual level, LLM agents learn a local utility function from exploratory datasets to better comprehend the embodied environment, which is then queried during test time to support informed decision-making. At the team level, LLM agents collaboratively and iteratively maintain and update a shared cooperation knowledge list based on new experiences, using it to guide more effective communication. By combining individual learning with team evolution, LIET enables comprehensive and flexible adaptation for LLM agents. Our experiments on Communicative Watch-And-Help and ThreeD-World Multi-Agent Transport benchmarks demonstrate that LIET, instantiated with both LLaMA and GPT-4o, outperforms existing baselines and exhibits strong cooperative planning abilities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework, "Learn as Individuals, Evolve as a Team" (LIET), to improve the adaptation of multi-agent Large Language Models (LLMs) in embodied environments. LIET addresses the limitations of directly using pre-trained LLMs for multi-agent planning by incorporating both individual learning and team evolution. Individually, agents learn a local utility function through fine-tuning on exploratory datasets to better understand the cost of actions in the environment. As a team, agents maintain and update a shared cooperation knowledge list based on experience, which guides their communication strategy and fosters more effective collaboration. The framework is evaluated on benchmarks like Communicative Watch-And-Help (C-WAH) and ThreeDWorld Multi-Agent Transport (TDW-MAT) using LLaMA and GPT-40, demonstrating significant performance gains over existing baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a relatively novel approach to multi-agent LLM planning. The combination of individual utility learning with a team-level evolving communication strategy differentiates it from prior work that often focuses on modular design or centralized communication.  The idea of using exploratory data to learn utility functions for cost estimation is a practical and valuable contribution. The dynamic prompting mechanism for communication, updated through a reflection module, is also a novel aspect.

*   **Significance:**  The work addresses a crucial limitation of current LLM-based planning methods: their weak adaptation to embodied multi-agent settings. Overcoming this limitation is essential for deploying these systems in real-world applications. The empirical results convincingly demonstrate that LIET improves performance significantly on challenging benchmarks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear and well-structured presentation.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation, including comparisons to several strong baselines and ablation studies.
    *   The visualization in the appendix provides valuable insights into the agents' reasoning and communication processes.
    *   The framework is flexible and can be used with different LLMs and extended to multi-agent scenarios (more than 2 agents).
    *   The individual cost function learning enables efficient local decisions, and the collaborative framework promotes team-wide coordination.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the framework shows good performance, there may be a limitation in generalizing results directly to real-world robotic tasks, mainly due to reliance on visual information being abstracted to text through pretrained vision models. The paper acknowledges this limitation, but more discussion on how to overcome it in future work would be beneficial.
    *   The evaluation relies on simulated environments, which may not fully capture the complexities and uncertainties of real-world settings. Although the LLaMAR work is cited, it would strengthen the paper to consider and discuss approaches for handling imperfect knowledge and more realistic sensor data.
    *   The prompt templates (provided in the Appendix) seem heavily engineered for the specific environments. A discussion on the robustness of the framework when faced with different tasks or more free-form task descriptions would be valuable.
    *   More detailed insight into the reflection process for the receiver would be valuable. Are there any examples of how the reflection changes the coorporation knowledge list?

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in multi-agent LLM planning by highlighting the importance of adaptation. The proposed LIET framework provides a practical and effective approach for improving collaboration in embodied environments. The concepts of individual utility learning and evolving communication strategies could inspire new directions in the field. The semi-centralized approach could be adopted by other researchers looking for a balance between flexibility and efficiency.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a strong and original contribution to multi-agent LLM planning. The LIET framework effectively addresses the crucial issue of adaptation in embodied environments. The experimental results are compelling, and the analysis provides valuable insights. While the evaluation is limited to simulated environments and specific task descriptions, the core ideas are transferable and can potentially be extended to more realistic settings. Taking these factors into account, the innovation is substantial, but more investigation into generalization would improve the score.

### Multi-Step Visual Reasoning with Visual Tokens Scaling and Verification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07235v1)
- **Authors**: Tianyi Bai, Zengjie Hu, Fupeng Sun, Jiantao Qiu, Yizhen Jiang, Guangxin He, Bohan Zeng, Conghui He, Binhang Yuan, Wentao Zhang
- **Abstract**: Multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) have achieved remarkable capabilities by integrating visual perception with language understanding, enabling applications such as image-grounded dialogue, visual question answering, and scientific analysis. However, most MLLMs adopt a static inference paradigm, encoding the entire image into fixed visual tokens upfront, which limits their ability to iteratively refine understanding or adapt to context during inference. This contrasts sharply with human perception, which is dynamic, selective, and feedback-driven. In this work, we introduce a novel framework for inference-time visual token scaling that enables MLLMs to perform iterative, verifier-guided reasoning over visual content. We formulate the problem as a Markov Decision Process, involving a reasoner that proposes visual actions and a verifier, which is trained via multi-step Direct Preference Optimization (DPO), that evaluates these actions and determines when reasoning should terminate. To support this, we present a new dataset, VTS, comprising supervised reasoning trajectories (VTS-SFT) and preference-labeled reasoning comparisons (VTS-DPO). Our method significantly outperforms existing approaches across diverse visual reasoning benchmarks, offering not only improved accuracy but also more interpretable and grounded reasoning processes. These results demonstrate the promise of dynamic inference mechanisms for enabling fine-grained, context-aware visual reasoning in next-generation MLLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Multi-Step Visual Reasoning with Visual Tokens Scaling and Verification":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the limitations of current Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in visual reasoning due to their static inference paradigm, where the entire image is encoded into fixed visual tokens upfront. To overcome this, the authors introduce a novel framework for inference-time visual token scaling that enables MLLMs to perform iterative, verifier-guided reasoning over visual content. They formulate the problem as a Markov Decision Process (MDP) with a reasoner (proposing visual actions) and a verifier (evaluating actions, trained using multi-step Direct Preference Optimization (DPO)). The paper also presents a new dataset, VTS, comprising supervised reasoning trajectories (VTS-SFT) and preference-labeled reasoning comparisons (VTS-DPO) to facilitate training. Experimental results on diverse visual reasoning benchmarks demonstrate significant improvements over existing approaches, alongside more interpretable reasoning processes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper presents a novel framework for dynamic visual token scaling within MLLMs.  The key innovations include:

*   **Iterative Reasoning:** The core idea of allowing the model to interactively refine its visual understanding is a significant departure from the static approach of most MLLMs.
*   **MDP Formulation:** Framing visual reasoning as an MDP provides a structured approach to action selection and termination.
*   **Verifier with DPO:** Using a verifier trained with multi-step DPO to guide reasoning is a sound approach, leveraging preference learning to improve action selection and determine when reasoning should stop.
*   **VTS Dataset:** The creation of a tailored dataset (VTS) containing both supervised trajectories and preference data directly addresses the lack of suitable training data for this type of iterative visual reasoning.

The combination of these elements demonstrates significant novelty.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance lies in its potential to unlock more sophisticated visual reasoning capabilities in MLLMs. Current MLLMs often struggle with tasks requiring fine-grained visual analysis or context-aware interpretation. By enabling dynamic visual token scaling, the proposed framework allows models to:

*   **Focus attention:** Selectively process relevant regions of an image, improving efficiency and accuracy.
*   **Adapt to context:** Refine their understanding of the visual scene based on the ongoing reasoning process.
*   **Handle ambiguity:**  Recover from initial misinterpretations by re-examining the image with different tools or perspectives.

The demonstrated improvements on challenging benchmarks (BLINK, V\*Bench, MMStar, MathVista) support these claims. The enhanced interpretability of the reasoning process is also valuable, providing insights into how the model arrives at its conclusions.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper effectively highlights the limitations of current MLLMs in visual reasoning.
*   **Well-Motivated Approach:** The proposed framework addresses the identified limitations in a logical and principled manner.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments cover a diverse set of visual reasoning tasks and strong baselines.
*   **Detailed Analysis:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contributions of visual token scaling and the verifier.
*   **Reproducibility:** The public release of code and data promotes reproducibility and future research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:** Dynamic token scaling can be computationally expensive.  The paper should discuss the computational overhead in more detail and explore potential optimization strategies.
*   **Generalization of Actions:** The action space is limited to the tools provided.  How can the framework be extended to allow the model to learn new tools or combine existing ones in novel ways?
*   **Dataset Dependence:** The framework relies on the VTS dataset for training.  How well would it generalize to other datasets or domains?
*   **Complexity of MDP:** The MDP formulation may add complexity to the models and also increase the overall memory footprint

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to significantly influence the development of future MLLMs.  By demonstrating the benefits of dynamic visual token scaling, it could encourage researchers to explore more interactive and context-aware inference mechanisms. The VTS dataset could also serve as a valuable resource for training and evaluating new visual reasoning models.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a significant advancement in visual reasoning for MLLMs. The proposed framework tackles a key limitation of existing models with a novel and well-designed approach. The creation of the VTS dataset and comprehensive experiments further strengthens the contribution. While there are some weaknesses related to computational cost and generalization, the potential impact on the field is considerable.

### SDE-SQL: Enhancing Text-to-SQL Generation in Large Language Models via Self-Driven Exploration with SQL Probes
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07245v1)
- **Authors**: Wenxuan Xie, Yaxun Dai, Wenhao Jiang
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in large language models (LLMs) have significantly improved performance on the Text-to-SQL task. However, prior approaches typically rely on static, pre-processed database information provided at inference time, which limits the model's ability to fully understand the database contents. Without dynamic interaction, LLMs are constrained to fixed, human-provided context and cannot autonomously explore the underlying data. To address this limitation, we propose SDE-SQL, a framework that enables large language models to perform self-driven exploration of databases during inference. This is accomplished by generating and executing SQL probes, which allow the model to actively retrieve information from the database and iteratively update its understanding of the data. Unlike prior methods, SDE-SQL operates in a zero-shot setting, without relying on any question-SQL pairs as in-context demonstrations. When evaluated on the BIRD benchmark with Qwen2.5-72B-Instruct, SDE-SQL achieves an 8.02% relative improvement in execution accuracy over the vanilla Qwen2.5-72B-Instruct baseline, establishing a new state-of-the-art among methods based on open-source models without supervised fine-tuning (SFT) or model ensembling. Moreover, with SFT, the performance of SDE-SQL can be further enhanced, yielding an additional 0.52% improvement.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SDE-SQL: Enhancing Text-to-SQL Generation in Large Language Models via Self-Driven Exploration with SQL Probes":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SDE-SQL, a framework that allows Large Language Models (LLMs) to actively explore databases during the Text-to-SQL task.  Unlike traditional methods that rely on static, pre-processed database information, SDE-SQL enables LLMs to generate and execute SQL "probes" to retrieve information, update their understanding of the data, and refine their SQL generation process.  The framework incorporates self-driven exploration in both the generation and refinement stages.  The approach includes entity-based schema linking, two-phase exploration with SQL probes, and a rule-based SQL refinement strategy based on Sub-SQLs. Evaluated on the BIRD benchmark, SDE-SQL achieves state-of-the-art performance among open-source models without ensembling, further improved with supervised fine-tuning (SFT).

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core idea of self-driven exploration using SQL probes is a valuable contribution. While previous works focused on prompt engineering, schema linking, and SQL refinement, SDE-SQL directly addresses the limitation of LLMs being confined to static database context. The two-phase exploration strategy and the decomposition-based refinement approach are also reasonably novel. The use of SQL as a feedback mechanism *within* the generation/refinement process, rather than simply as a final output, distinguishes this work from many existing approaches. The idea of using the SQL engine itself to explore data semantics has merit.

**Significance:**  The potential impact of SDE-SQL is significant. By enabling LLMs to dynamically interact with databases, the framework reduces the performance gap between LLM-based systems and human experts. The improvements on the challenging BIRD dataset further highlight the practicality of the approach. This could lead to more robust and accurate Text-to-SQL systems in real-world scenarios, especially those involving complex and messy databases. The reduction of reliance on static information makes the solution more adaptable and less prone to errors introduced by outdated or incomplete schemas. However, it is important to consider the resource implications (e.g., query costs on cloud databases). The analysis of errors and how different probe strategies lead to error recovery is also a plus, as the understanding of what makes exploration valuable to the models should be explored.

**Strengths:**

*   **Addressing a key limitation:** Tackles the problem of static context in Text-to-SQL by enabling dynamic database interaction.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:**  Demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on the BIRD benchmark for open-source models (without ensembling or extensive fine-tuning), and achieves competitive results on the Spider dataset.
*   **Ablation Studies:**  Provides detailed ablation studies to evaluate the contribution of each component. This helps to justify the claims.
*   **Comprehensive Framework:**  Integrates self-driven exploration into both generation and refinement stages.
*   **Well-written and clear:** The paper is generally well-structured and easy to understand.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Prompt Engineering Dependency:** While the *idea* of database exploration is valuable, the *implementation* relies heavily on carefully crafted prompts. The paper acknowledges this in the "Limitation" section. The quality and design of SQL probe prompts remain critical, and the framework offers limited adaptivity in this area. The templates need to be more adaptive to different databases and query styles.
*   **Resource Intensive:** Generating and executing multiple SQL probes can be computationally expensive, especially for large databases. The paper doesn't adequately address the resource implications. This limits the practical scalability and wider adoption of the approach. Query optimization will be crucial for real-world use.
*   **Limited Generalization Assessment:** While results on BIRD and Spider are compelling, the paper provides limited information about how SDE-SQL generalizes to other databases and query types. Specifically, the transferability of the SQL Probe prompt design needs to be investigated.
*   **Rule-based Decomposition:** The refinement stage uses rule-based decomposition, which may not be optimal for all types of errors or SQL queries. While rule-based systems offer deterministic behaviour, they lack the generalisation of a learned strategy.
*   **Incremental Improvement over Baselines:** The improvements over strong baselines may not be considered "exceptional." While statistically significant, the magnitude of the increase needs to be balanced with the added complexity and cost of SDE-SQL.
*   **Lack of theoretical explanation for successful strategy and parameters:** While extensive experiments are performed, the parameters for the SQL Probes (e.g. number of probes) and criteria that determine whether to modify SQL queries do not have a strong theoretical foundation.

**Justification for Score:**

SDE-SQL introduces a significant and valuable concept – empowering LLMs to dynamically explore databases during Text-to-SQL. The demonstrated improvements on complex datasets like BIRD further validate its practicality. However, the strong dependence on prompt engineering, potential resource intensity, limitations in generalization evaluation, and the incremental improvements over baseline methods temper the overall impact.

Considering both the novelty of the concept and the potential for future research, I would assign a score of **7**. This reflects the paper's contribution as a promising approach to overcome a core limitation in existing Text-to-SQL systems, while acknowledging areas that warrant further refinement and investigation. The prompt dependence and resource overhead reduce the impact slightly, however the core idea presents good direction for future work.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Stable Whitening Optimizer for Efficient Neural Network Training
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07254v1)
- **Authors**: Kevin Frans, Sergey Levine, Pieter Abbeel
- **Abstract**: In this work, we take an experimentally grounded look at neural network optimization. Building on the Shampoo family of algorithms, we identify and alleviate three key issues, resulting in the proposed SPlus method. First, we find that naive Shampoo is prone to divergence when matrix-inverses are cached for long periods. We introduce an alternate bounded update combining a historical eigenbasis with instantaneous normalization, resulting in across-the-board stability and significantly lower computational requirements. Second, we adapt a shape-aware scaling to enable learning rate transfer across network width. Third, we find that high learning rates result in large parameter noise, and propose a simple iterate-averaging scheme which unblocks faster learning. To properly confirm these findings, we introduce a pointed Transformer training benchmark, considering three objectives (language modelling, image classification, and diffusion modelling) across different stages of training. On average, SPlus is able to reach the validation performance of Adam within 44% of the gradient steps and 62% of the wallclock time.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SPlus, a new optimization algorithm for training neural networks, particularly Transformers. SPlus builds upon the Shampoo family of optimizers, addressing three key issues identified through empirical analysis: divergence caused by long-cached matrix inverses, improper scaling of update magnitudes with network width, and high parameter noise due to large learning rates. SPlus tackles these issues by employing instant-sign normalization, shape-aware scaling, and iterate averaging. The authors demonstrate that SPlus achieves faster convergence and better stability compared to Adam and other optimizers across various Transformer training tasks (language modeling, image classification, and diffusion modeling), measured in terms of gradient steps and wall-clock time. The paper provides open-source code for reproducibility and ease of adoption.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents a solid engineering contribution built upon existing optimization techniques.

*   **Instant-Sign Normalization:** The replacement of the square-root matrix inverse with instant-sign normalization is a clever way to enhance stability and allow for less frequent inversions. While sign descent alone is not new (as the authors acknowledge), its use within a Shampoo-like framework, coupled with the empirical validation, offers a practical improvement.
*   **Shape-Aware Scaling:** The adaptation of scaling methods from SGD and Adam to the Kronecker-factored setting is a useful refinement, enabling more consistent learning rate behavior across different network widths. The ablation studies justifying symmetric scaling are valuable.
*   **Iterate Averaging:** While iterate averaging is not a novel concept itself, its specific application to mitigate parameter noise in whitening-based optimizers and enable higher learning rates is a relevant and practical contribution.

The paper's true novelty lies in the *combination* of these techniques within a carefully designed and validated framework.

**Significance:** The paper offers a valuable contribution to the practical aspects of neural network training.

*   **Performance Improvement:** The consistent demonstration of faster convergence compared to Adam on diverse tasks and at different training stages is significant. This can lead to tangible benefits in terms of reduced training time and computational costs.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The thorough experimental setup, considering multiple objectives, datasets, and checkpoints, strengthens the claims and provides a more comprehensive view of SPlus's performance. This contrasts with many optimization papers that focus on a single task.
*   **Ease of Use:** The emphasis on simplicity and the provision of open-source code make SPlus readily accessible to practitioners, potentially facilitating its wider adoption.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** The paper is primarily driven by empirical observations. While the authors offer intuitive explanations for the issues they address, a more rigorous theoretical analysis of SPlus's convergence properties would be beneficial.
*   **Computational Cost:** While SPlus demonstrates better wall-clock time compared to Adam, it still involves Kronecker factorization and eigendecomposition which inherently are computationally more expensive than Adam. Although the algorithm is faster, it is not a drastic improvement from prior optimizers and introduces new engineering challenges.
*   **Marginal gains over other recent optimizers:** SPlus has some advantages over some common optimizers, it is unclear that it is much better than other recent optimizers such as Sophia.
*   **Limited architecture focus** By design, the paper focuses on the transformer architecture, while the transformer is widely used today, the lack of consideration of other neural network architectures limits its scope.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper delivers a tangible improvement in optimizer performance and offers valuable practical insights. The strong empirical validation and open-source availability increase its potential impact. However, the incremental nature of the individual techniques, and the lack of a deeper theoretical analysis, limit its overall novelty. While it is a significant contribution to the engineering aspect of optimization, it doesn't represent a groundbreaking theoretical advancement. The computational costs of SPlus are higher than Adam, but this is balanced by the overall convergence speed in some cases.

Therefore, the paper merits a score of **7**.  It's a well-executed and practically relevant contribution to the field of neural network optimization, but the limited theoretical novelty prevents it from being a truly exceptional work.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Question Answering under Temporal Conflict: Evaluating and Organizing Evolving Knowledge with LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07270v1)
- **Authors**: Atahan Özer, Çağatay Yıldız
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) exhibit remarkable capabilities in question answering and reasoning thanks to their extensive parametric memory. However, their knowledge is inherently limited by the scope of their pre-training data, while real-world information evolves continuously. Updating this knowledge typically requires costly and brittle re-training, or in-context learning (ICL), which becomes impractical at scale given the volume and volatility of modern information. Motivated by these limitations, we investigate how LLMs perform when exposed to temporal text corpora, or documents that reflect evolving knowledge over time, such as sports biographies where facts like a player's "current team" change year by year. To this end, we introduce two new benchmarks: Temporal Wiki, which captures factual drift across historical Wikipedia snapshots, and Unified Clark, which aggregates timestamped news articles to simulate real-world information accumulation. Our analysis reveals that LLMs often struggle to reconcile conflicting or outdated facts and can be misled when multiple versions of a fact appear in context. To address these issues, we propose a lightweight, agentic framework that incrementally builds a structured, external memory from source documents without requiring re-training. This knowledge organization strategy enables models to retrieve and reason over temporally filtered, relevant information at inference time. Empirically, our method outperforms ICL and RAG baselines across both benchmarks, especially on questions requiring more complex reasoning or integration of conflicting facts.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of question answering (QA) when dealing with temporally evolving knowledge in Large Language Models (LLMs).  LLMs are limited by their training data's scope and cutoff dates.  Updating knowledge often involves costly retraining or impractical in-context learning (ICL).  The authors investigate LLMs' performance on temporal text corpora and introduce two new benchmarks: *Temporal Wiki* (factual drift across Wikipedia snapshots) and *Unified Clark* (timestamped news articles).  Their analysis shows LLMs struggle to reconcile conflicting or outdated facts and can be misled by multiple versions of a fact in context. To mitigate this, they propose a lightweight, agentic framework that incrementally builds a structured, external memory from source documents *without* retraining. This facilitates temporally filtered information retrieval and reasoning.  Empirically, their method outperforms ICL and Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) baselines, especially on questions requiring complex reasoning or integration of conflicting facts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the introduction of the two new benchmarks (*Temporal Wiki* and *Unified Clark*) explicitly designed to evaluate LLMs' ability to handle temporal knowledge drift and conflict. While some prior work has touched upon temporal aspects of language models and knowledge bases, the authors' benchmarks provide a more focused and structured evaluation environment. The proposed "knowledge organization" framework is also a useful contribution, demonstrating a viable alternative to full retraining or simply throwing a large context window at the LLM. However, the framework in itself is an incremental improvement to existing methods, it is not a fundamentally new concept.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the increasing importance of dealing with temporal aspects in knowledge-intensive NLP tasks. As LLMs are deployed in real-world applications requiring up-to-date information, robust mechanisms for handling evolving knowledge are crucial. The paper's findings highlight the limitations of existing ICL and RAG approaches in such scenarios and demonstrate the potential of structured, external memory systems to improve performance.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined problem statement:  Clearly articulates the problem of temporal knowledge drift in LLMs.
    *   New benchmarks: *Temporal Wiki* and *Unified Clark* provide valuable resources for future research.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation: Experiments compare several models and approaches, revealing clear performance trends.
    *   Practical solution:  The proposed knowledge organization framework offers a practical and efficient way to mitigate temporal conflict.
    *  The writing is exceptionally clear and the experimental setup is reproducible.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Framework complexity: The "knowledge organization" framework, while effective, might introduce overhead and requires careful design of the external memory structure. However, this issue is clearly mentioned in the paper and is a trade-off to improve performance.
    *   Limited scope: The experiments focus on specific types of questions and temporal conflicts. It is not clear how well the proposed framework would generalize to other types of temporal reasoning or more complex knowledge domains.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper is likely to influence future research in several ways:
    *   It will encourage the development of more sophisticated evaluation benchmarks for temporal knowledge reasoning.
    *   It will motivate further exploration of structured, external memory systems for LLMs.
    *   It will highlight the importance of addressing knowledge drift and conflict in real-world LLM applications.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7**. The paper makes a solid contribution by introducing useful benchmarks and demonstrating the limitations of existing approaches. The proposed knowledge organization method is a practical and effective solution, but it is not a groundbreaking innovation. The impact will be primarily in the form of influencing future research directions and providing a valuable resource for the community, rather than fundamentally changing the field's trajectory. The methodology and implementation are very well designed.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Parsing the Switch: LLM-Based UD Annotation for Complex Code-Switched and Low-Resource Languages
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07274v1)
- **Authors**: Olga Kellert, Nemika Tyagi, Muhammad Imran, Nelvin Licona-Guevara, Carlos Gómez-Rodríguez
- **Abstract**: Code-switching presents a complex challenge for syntactic analysis, especially in low-resource language settings where annotated data is scarce. While recent work has explored the use of large language models (LLMs) for sequence-level tagging, few approaches systematically investigate how well these models capture syntactic structure in code-switched contexts. Moreover, existing parsers trained on monolingual treebanks often fail to generalize to multilingual and mixed-language input. To address this gap, we introduce the BiLingua Parser, an LLM-based annotation pipeline designed to produce Universal Dependencies (UD) annotations for code-switched text. First, we develop a prompt-based framework for Spanish-English and Spanish-Guaran\'i data, combining few-shot LLM prompting with expert review. Second, we release two annotated datasets, including the first Spanish-Guaran\'i UD-parsed corpus. Third, we conduct a detailed syntactic analysis of switch points across language pairs and communicative contexts. Experimental results show that BiLingua Parser achieves up to 95.29% LAS after expert revision, significantly outperforming prior baselines and multilingual parsers. These results show that LLMs, when carefully guided, can serve as practical tools for bootstrapping syntactic resources in under-resourced, code-switched environments. Data and source code are available at https://github.com/N3mika/ParsingProject
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces BiLingua Parser, an LLM-based pipeline designed to generate Universal Dependencies (UD) annotations for code-switched text, focusing on Spanish-English and Spanish-Guaraní. The authors develop a prompt-based framework using few-shot LLM prompting with expert review, and they release two annotated datasets, including the first Spanish-Guaraní UD-parsed corpus. A detailed syntactic analysis of switch points across language pairs and communicative contexts is performed.  The results show that BiLingua Parser achieves up to 95.29% LAS after expert revision, outperforming existing baselines and multilingual parsers. The paper argues that LLMs, when guided carefully, can be used to bootstrap syntactic resources in under-resourced, code-switched environments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in several key areas: 1) Introducing an LLM-based approach for UD annotation of code-switched data; 2) Creating and releasing new UD-annotated datasets for Spanish-English and, significantly, Spanish-Guaraní (a low-resource pair); 3) Performing a detailed syntactic analysis of switch points beyond POS tags, focusing on dependency relations; and 4)  Demonstrating that careful LLM prompting and expert validation can produce high-quality syntactic annotations, even exceeding traditional methods.
