- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Return of ChebNet: Understanding and Improving an Overlooked GNN on Long Range Tasks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07624v1)
- **Authors**: Ali Hariri, Álvaro Arroyo, Alessio Gravina, Moshe Eliasof, Carola-Bibiane Schönlieb, Davide Bacciu, Kamyar Azizzadenesheli, Xiaowen Dong, Pierre Vandergheynst
- **Abstract**: ChebNet, one of the earliest spectral GNNs, has largely been overshadowed by Message Passing Neural Networks (MPNNs), which gained popularity for their simplicity and effectiveness in capturing local graph structure. Despite their success, MPNNs are limited in their ability to capture long-range dependencies between nodes. This has led researchers to adapt MPNNs through rewiring or make use of Graph Transformers, which compromises the computational efficiency that characterized early spatial message-passing architectures, and typically disregards the graph structure. Almost a decade after its original introduction, we revisit ChebNet to shed light on its ability to model distant node interactions. We find that out-of-box, ChebNet already shows competitive advantages relative to classical MPNNs and GTs on long-range benchmarks, while maintaining good scalability properties for high-order polynomials. However, we uncover that this polynomial expansion leads ChebNet to an unstable regime during training. To address this limitation, we cast ChebNet as a stable and non-dissipative dynamical system, which we coin Stable-ChebNet. Our Stable-ChebNet model allows for stable information propagation, and has controllable dynamics which do not require the use of eigendecompositions, positional encodings, or graph rewiring. Across several benchmarks, Stable-ChebNet achieves near state-of-the-art performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper revisits ChebNet, an early spectral graph neural network (GNN), arguing it has been overlooked despite its potential for modeling long-range dependencies in graphs. While ChebNet demonstrates advantages over Message Passing Neural Networks (MPNNs) and Graph Transformers on long-range benchmarks, it suffers from training instability due to its polynomial expansion. The authors address this by reformulating ChebNet as a stable dynamical system, termed Stable-ChebNet, which ensures stable information propagation without requiring eigendecompositions, positional encodings, or graph rewiring. Empirical results across several benchmarks show Stable-ChebNet achieving near state-of-the-art performance. The authors provide theoretical analysis regarding ChebNet's stability and provide a practical solution via their Stable-ChebNet framework.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies in several aspects. Firstly, it shines a light on the often forgotten ChebNet, demonstrating that it can be a competitive alternative, especially for long-range graph tasks. Secondly, the authors perform an in-depth analysis of the original ChebNet architecture, showing that simply increasing the polynomial order leads to instability. Thirdly, they propose a novel Stable-ChebNet formulation by recasting it into a continuous-time ODE with antisymmetric weight constraints. This is important since it not only provides a stable architecture but also offers a connection to stable dynamical systems. Finally, the implementation of stable numerical discretization gives a simple and effective method for maintaining stable information propagation.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is multifold. It offers a scalable alternative to address limitations of MPNNs by leveraging spectral approaches. Addressing the instability of ChebNet is a crucial step in making it a practical choice. The Stable-ChebNet formulation, based on a sound theoretical understanding, provides a computationally efficient option for long-range graph modeling. The experimental results on benchmark datasets further validate the efficacy of the proposed method. Finally, the work also serves as a reminder that older techniques should not be immediately discarded, especially when they possess unique features that can potentially resolve some issues.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Strong theoretical foundation: The paper provides a rigorous analysis of ChebNet's dynamics and the conditions under which it becomes unstable.
    *   Effective solution: Stable-ChebNet addresses the instability issue in a principled way, leading to improved performance.
    *   Scalability:  The proposed approach maintains the scalability advantages of ChebNet, making it applicable to large graphs.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation: The experiments cover a range of datasets and tasks, demonstrating the robustness of Stable-ChebNet.
    *   Clear writing and presentation: The paper is well-written and easy to follow, with clear explanations of the technical concepts.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While showing stability on the benchmarks, the paper does not provide any guarantees on all possible datasets.
    *   The paper states that the Euler step not only addresses the shortcomings of long-range information propagation but also performs effectively well on graphs with many nodes, however, it is not very thoroughly tested and compared, particularly on the OGB datasets.
    *   In some experiments, particularly in the Peptide experiments, Stable-ChebNet seems to be improving the performance of "long-range" information propagation, but falling a little short of other methods.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work could have a significant influence on the field of graph neural networks by encouraging researchers to revisit spectral methods and explore connections to dynamical systems. It may also lead to the development of new and improved GNN architectures that are both scalable and capable of modeling long-range dependencies.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** The paper makes a solid contribution to the GNN field by revisiting an overlooked architecture and providing a theoretically sound and empirically validated improvement. The focus on stability is a critical aspect often disregarded in spectral GNNs. However, despite these strengths, there is a slight decrease in performance for the peptide datasets, and stronger validation is necessary to prove stability of every dataset. Therefore, while promising and likely to be influential, the paper does have some limitations.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Intent Matters: Enhancing AI Tutoring with Fine-Grained Pedagogical Intent Annotation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07626v1)
- **Authors**: Kseniia Petukhova, Ekaterina Kochmar
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) hold great promise for educational applications, particularly in intelligent tutoring systems. However, effective tutoring requires alignment with pedagogical strategies - something current LLMs lack without task-specific adaptation. In this work, we explore whether fine-grained annotation of teacher intents can improve the quality of LLM-generated tutoring responses. We focus on MathDial, a dialog dataset for math instruction, and apply an automated annotation framework to re-annotate a portion of the dataset using a detailed taxonomy of eleven pedagogical intents. We then fine-tune an LLM using these new annotations and compare its performance to models trained on the original four-category taxonomy. Both automatic and qualitative evaluations show that the fine-grained model produces more pedagogically aligned and effective responses. Our findings highlight the value of intent specificity for controlled text generation in educational settings, and we release our annotated data and code to facilitate further research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the impact of fine-grained teacher intent annotation on the quality of LLM-generated tutoring responses in the MathDial dataset. It expands the original four-category taxonomy of teacher moves to an eleven-intent taxonomy and utilizes an automated annotation framework to re-annotate a portion of the dataset.  The authors then fine-tune a Mistral-7B-Instruct model on both the original and re-annotated data and compare their performance using both automatic metrics and human evaluation. The results demonstrate that the model trained on the finer-grained intent annotation produces more pedagogically aligned and effective responses. The authors release their annotated data and code to promote further research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in applying a previously established automated annotation framework to re-annotate an existing educational dialogue dataset with a more detailed, fine-grained taxonomy of teacher intents.  While LLMs have been used in educational settings and controlled generation is also a well-known technique, the specific combination of automated fine-grained annotation for pedagogical intent and its impact on the quality of LLM-generated tutoring responses is a reasonable contribution. While related work exists in using LLMs for education, the specific focus on *intent* is a differentiating factor. The automated re-annotation using a tree-based approach is also valuable.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant for several reasons:
    *   It demonstrates the value of detailed pedagogical intent annotation in improving the quality of AI tutoring systems. This is important because effective tutoring requires more than just fluent conversation, it needs a deep understanding of pedagogical strategies.
    *   It provides a practical approach to creating high-quality annotated datasets for educational applications using automated methods, which can reduce the cost and time associated with manual annotation.
    *   The released dataset and code can serve as a valuable resource for researchers in AI education and controlled text generation.
    *   The paper identifies inconsistencies in the original MathDial dataset, suggesting areas for improvement in data collection and annotation procedures.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The methodology is sound and clearly described. The authors utilize appropriate evaluation metrics and conduct both automatic and human evaluations.
    *   The results are well-presented and support the paper's claims.  The human evaluation reinforces the advantages of the finer-grained model.
    *   The paper addresses a practical problem in AI education – the need for pedagogically aligned AI tutors.
    *   The open-source release of data and code increases the reproducibility and impact of the work.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The scale of the evaluation is somewhat limited. The human evaluation is based on a small number of dialogues (30 response pairs).
    *   While the authors acknowledge this limitation, the fine-tuning is done with a small language model. The improvement might not generalize well to larger, more sophisticated models.
    *   The reliance on reference-based metrics can be problematic in open-ended generation tasks. The responses are diverse and human-like and thus these metrics might not be the best way to evaluate.
    *   The inter-annotator agreement in the human evaluation is only "fair," suggesting that the judgment of "better" is highly subjective.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:
    *   Encouraging the development of more sophisticated annotation schemes for educational dialogue datasets.
    *   Promoting the use of automated annotation methods for creating large-scale datasets for AI education.
    *   Inspiring further research on controlled generation techniques for AI tutoring.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning this paper a score of **7/10**. The work makes a valuable contribution by showing that detailed annotation of teacher intent can improve LLM-based tutoring systems. While the re-annotation step and the results confirm this hypothesis in the MathDial dataset, this work is an important step towards building better AI tutors. The methodological soundness is a strength, as is the release of resources.  However, the relatively small scale of the human evaluation, limitations of model and evaluation metrics, and the "fair" agreement in the human evaluation bring the novelty a bit down. Nevertheless, this provides a clear research avenue for improving AI tutoring and provides a framework for building more effective intent-driven educational dialogues.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### SongBloom: Coherent Song Generation via Interleaved Autoregressive Sketching and Diffusion Refinement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07634v1)
- **Authors**: Chenyu Yang, Shuai Wang, Hangting Chen, Wei Tan, Jianwei Yu, Haizhou Li
- **Abstract**: Generating music with coherent structure, harmonious instrumental and vocal elements remains a significant challenge in song generation. Existing language models and diffusion-based methods often struggle to balance global coherence with local fidelity, resulting in outputs that lack musicality or suffer from incoherent progression and mismatched lyrics. This paper introduces $\textbf{SongBloom}$, a novel framework for full-length song generation that leverages an interleaved paradigm of autoregressive sketching and diffusion-based refinement. SongBloom employs an autoregressive diffusion model that combines the high fidelity of diffusion models with the scalability of language models. Specifically, it gradually extends a musical sketch from short to long and refines the details from coarse to fine-grained. The interleaved generation paradigm effectively integrates prior semantic and acoustic context to guide the generation process. Experimental results demonstrate that SongBloom outperforms existing methods across both subjective and objective metrics and achieves performance comparable to the state-of-the-art commercial music generation platforms. Audio samples are available on our demo page: https://cypress-yang.github.io/SongBloom\_demo.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SongBloom, a new framework for full-length song generation.  It uses an interleaved paradigm of autoregressive sketching and diffusion-based refinement. SongBloom combines the high fidelity of diffusion models with the scalability of language models using an autoregressive diffusion model.  The system generates music in two stages: first, it sketches the song in a coarse manner using an autoregressive model, and then it refines the details using a diffusion model. The interleaved approach alternates between these two stages, using both semantic and acoustic context to guide the generation process. Experiments show SongBloom outperforms existing methods in subjective and objective metrics and approaches the quality of commercial music generation platforms like Suno.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents several novel aspects. The core idea of interleaved autoregressive sketching and diffusion refinement is a solid architectural contribution.  The integration of sketch tokens as CoT-like prompts for acoustic generation is also interesting. While two-stage generation is not entirely new (MusicLM, YuE, SongEditor), the interleaved approach is a significant refinement.  The paper presents the first autoregressive diffusion model specifically designed for full-length *song* generation (as opposed to shorter music snippets or speech).

**Significance:**  The significance stems from the improved coherence and musicality reported compared to existing open-source methods. The claims of approaching commercial system quality are also important, although limited without access to ground-truth and code implementations. Specifically, demonstrating an improved trade-off between generation quality and computational efficiency (lower RTF) is a valuable step towards practical music generation systems. The paper addresses a key challenge in long-form music generation: maintaining both global coherence and local fidelity. A weakness is that the evaluation partly compares the model against commercial platforms.

**Strengths:**
*   **Novel Architecture:** The interleaved autoregressive-diffusion approach with CoT-like sketch tokens is a good conceptual contribution.
*   **Strong Performance:** The experimental results indicate significant improvements over open-source baselines and competitive performance against (unavailable for inspection) commercial systems.
*   **Improved Coherence and Musicality:** Addresses a well-known limitation of previous methods.
*   **Computational Efficiency:** Claims and demonstrates a lower RTF than other LM-based methods.

**Weaknesses:**
*   **Limited Evaluation Against Commercial Systems:** Evaluation is based on perceptual qualities and automated metrics; code isn't released, and there is reliance on closed-source, unavailable models as comparisons. The field is moving towards open source and reproducible models.
*   **Sketch Representation:** The sketch representation is based on representations from SSL models lacking interpretability limiting control.
*   **Dataset Details:** While the dataset size is mentioned (100K hours), more details on the dataset composition and potential biases could strengthen the paper.
*   **Ethical Considerations:** The paper touches upon ethical considerations but could delve deeper into the potential for misuse and the steps taken to mitigate copyright issues.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper introduces a novel architecture with results showing a significant leap in quality for full song generation. This is a very difficult problem and a popular application area, indicating significance in the field.  The limitations are the reliance on comparison to non-public models, and an unreleased code base. The strengths outweigh the weaknesses, demonstrating a meaningful step forward for the community.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: eess.AS
- **Score**: 7/10

### SWE-Dev: Building Software Engineering Agents with Training and Inference Scaling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07636v1)
- **Authors**: Haoran Wang, Zhenyu Hou, Yao Wei, Jie Tang, Yuxiao Dong
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have advanced rapidly from conversational problem solving to addressing real-world tasks involving tool use, such as software engineering (SWE). Recent LLM-powered toolkits, such as OpenAI Codex and Cursor, have offered end-to-end automation of the software development process. However, building effective SWE agents remains challenging due to the lack of high-quality training data and effective test cases. To address this issue, we present SWE-Dev, an SWE agent built upon open-source LLMs. First, we develop a robust pipeline to synthesize test cases for patch evaluation. Second, we scale up agent trajectories to construct the training data for building SWE-Dev. Experiments on the SWE-bench-Verified benchmark show that the SWE-Dev models can achieve top performance among all open SWE agents. Specifically, the success rates of the SWE-Dev 7B and 32B parameter models reach 23.4% and 36.6%, respectively, outperforming state-of-the-art open-source models. All code, models, and datasets are publicly available at https://github.com/THUDM/SWE-Dev.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SWE-Dev: Building Software Engineering Agents with Training and Inference Scaling":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SWE-DEV, an open-source framework for building Software Engineering (SWE) agents using Large Language Models (LLMs). It addresses the challenges of training effective SWE agents by focusing on both training and inference scaling. For training, they develop a pipeline to synthesize test cases and scale up agent trajectories for data generation. For inference, they increase the interaction budget within a single run to allow for more in-depth reasoning. Experiments on SWE-bench-Verified demonstrate that SWE-DEV models achieve state-of-the-art performance among open-source SWE agents. The authors also explore techniques like Rejection Sampling Fine-Tuning (RFT), Knowledge Transfer Optimization (KTO), and Offline Re-inforcement Optimization (OREO) to improve performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a well-engineered system and demonstrates strong empirical results, but the core ideas have some existing precedents. The test case generation pipeline, while valuable, builds upon existing work on generating code and tests. The combination of training and inference scaling is a good, practical approach. However, the individual components aren't radically new. The use of RFT, KTO, and OREO while adding value, is not novel in and of itself.
*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the thorough implementation and the demonstration of strong performance on a challenging benchmark. The open-source nature of SWE-DEV, along with the data generation pipeline, provides a valuable resource for the community to build upon and advance SWE agent research. While not fundamentally groundbreaking, it pushes the state-of-the-art for open-source models, narrowing the gap with closed-source systems. The detailed analysis of scaling trends in data and inference is also a valuable contribution, providing insights into the factors that influence agent performance.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong empirical results and state-of-the-art performance among open-source SWE agents.
    *   Open-source code, models, and datasets, making the work reproducible and accessible.
    *   Systematic approach to training and inference scaling.
    *   Comprehensive analysis of different training techniques (RFT, KTO, OREO).
    *   Focus on generating verifiable, test-passing solutions, a key requirement for real-world SWE tasks.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Incremental novelty in individual components. While the combination of ideas is effective, each component has existing precedents.
    *   The limitations of offline RL observed are not thoroughly addressed, hinting at a need for more specialized exploration strategies or more task-aligned reward signals.
*   **Potential Influence:** SWE-DEV has the potential to influence the field by providing a strong open-source baseline for SWE agent research. The data generation pipeline and scaling analysis can inform the design of future agents. The open-source nature of SWE-DEV would undoubtedly inspire the innovation and improvements across various domains in SW engineering.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper represents a solid engineering effort with significant practical value. While the individual components aren't groundbreaking in isolation, the combination of training and inference scaling, along with the rigorous empirical evaluation and open-source release, make it a valuable contribution. The score reflects the incremental novelty but significant practical impact and potential influence on the field, particularly within the open-source community.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Fast ECoT: Efficient Embodied Chain-of-Thought via Thoughts Reuse
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07639v1)
- **Authors**: Zhekai Duan, Yuan Zhang, Shikai Geng, Gaowen Liu, Joschka Boedecker, Chris Xiaoxuan Lu
- **Abstract**: Embodied Chain-of-Thought (ECoT) reasoning enhances vision-language-action (VLA) models by improving performance and interpretability through intermediate reasoning steps. However, its sequential autoregressive token generation introduces significant inference latency, limiting real-time deployment. We propose Fast ECoT, an inference-time acceleration method that exploits the structured and repetitive nature of ECoT to (1) cache and reuse high-level reasoning across timesteps and (2) parallelise the generation of modular reasoning steps. Additionally, we introduce an asynchronous scheduler that decouples reasoning from action decoding, further boosting responsiveness. Fast ECoT requires no model changes or additional training and integrates easily into existing VLA pipelines. Experiments in both simulation (LIBERO) and real-world robot tasks show up to a 7.5% reduction in latency with comparable or improved task success rate and reasoning faithfulness, bringing ECoT policies closer to practical real-time deployment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Fast ECoT: Efficient Embodied Chain-of-Thought via Thoughts Reuse" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the inference latency bottleneck in Embodied Chain-of-Thought (ECoT) reasoning for Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models. ECoT enhances VLA models by generating interpretable intermediate reasoning steps, but its sequential token generation leads to significant computational cost. The authors propose Fast ECoT, an inference-time acceleration method that leverages the structured and repetitive nature of ECoT outputs. Fast ECoT caches and reuses high-level reasoning across timesteps, parallelizes the generation of modular reasoning steps, and introduces an asynchronous scheduler to decouple reasoning from action decoding. The method requires no model changes or additional training and integrates into existing VLA pipelines. Experiments in simulation and real-world robot tasks demonstrate significant latency reduction with comparable or improved task success and reasoning faithfulness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in its practical approach to accelerating ECoT inference.  While chain-of-thought prompting and its embodied variant are established techniques, addressing the inference latency issue through thought reuse and partial parallelization is a novel and practical improvement.  The caching and parallelization strategies specifically exploit the structure inherent in *ECoT* traces, which is a clever observation.  The asynchronous scheduler is another valuable practical contribution. The work distinguishes itself from token-level optimization methods (e.g., speculative decoding, quantization) by operating at the reasoning-step level, exploiting the semantic structure of CoT outputs. This focus on *reasoning*-level acceleration is a differentiating factor.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a critical limitation of ECoT that hinders its real-world applicability. Reducing inference latency is crucial for robotic policies that need to react quickly to environmental changes. The experimental results demonstrating substantial latency reduction (up to 7.5x) without sacrificing performance are compelling. The method's ease of integration into existing pipelines (no retraining) further enhances its practical significance.  The real-world experiments are also important in showcasing the effectiveness of the approach beyond simulation.  However, the generalization to even *more* complex tasks/environments needs further validation.  Additionally, a deeper analysis of the trade-offs between latency reduction and reasoning faithfulness is warranted, particularly with the asynchronous variant. The evaluation focuses on a relatively limited set of robot manipulation tasks, and the long-term impact on policy learning and adaptation remains to be seen. Future work should explore the application of Fast ECoT to more complex and dynamic robotic tasks, such as navigation in cluttered environments or collaborative manipulation scenarios.

**Strengths:**

*   **Practical Solution:** Fast ECoT provides a practical and readily implementable solution to a significant problem in ECoT.
*   **Performance Improvement:** The experimental results demonstrate significant latency reduction with maintained or improved task success.
*   **Ease of Integration:** No model changes or additional training are required, facilitating easy adoption.
*   **Real-World Validation:** The paper presents compelling results in both simulation and real-world experiments.
*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the method and its benefits.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Generalization Assessment:** The evaluation focuses on a specific set of robot manipulation tasks; wider application scenarios need exploration.
*   **Reasoning Faithfulness Trade-offs:**  A more in-depth analysis of the impact of asynchronous scheduling on reasoning faithfulness is necessary. Specifically, when does async cause problems?
*   **Dependency on ECoT Structure:** The method's effectiveness relies on the relatively structured nature of ECoT reasoning traces.  Tasks with more dynamic reasoning requirements might not benefit as much.
*   **Incremental Innovation:** Although technically sound and practically relevant, the novelty is somewhat incremental, building upon existing techniques like ECoT and continuous batching.
*   **Limited Baselines:** The comparison to Quantized ECOT is good, but it would be insightful to compare with token level optimisation techniques (e.g., speculative decoding) to quantify benefits.
*   **Small Dataset:** The dataset used is fairly small and might impact generalizability.

**Overall:** The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of robot learning by addressing a critical limitation of ECoT. While the novelty may be somewhat incremental, the practical significance and performance improvements are substantial. The work demonstrates a strong understanding of the challenges associated with deploying complex reasoning systems in real-world robotic applications and provides a viable solution. The ease of integration and lack of retraining requirements make this a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners working with ECoT-based VLA models.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a practically valuable and well-executed method for accelerating ECoT reasoning. It demonstrates strong performance improvements and addresses a critical bottleneck in deploying ECoT-based policies in real-world robotic systems. However, the novelty is somewhat incremental, and the evaluation could benefit from a broader range of tasks and a deeper analysis of the trade-offs involved, or inclusion of more comparative baselines, to justify a higher score. A few caveats are noted in the critique regarding data volume and evaluation of the impact of asynchronous scheduling on reasoning faithfulness. These caveats are not detrimental, but do temper the overall score.

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### TreeReview: A Dynamic Tree of Questions Framework for Deep and Efficient LLM-based Scientific Peer Review
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07642v1)
- **Authors**: Yuan Chang, Ziyue Li, Hengyuan Zhang, Yuanbo Kong, Yanru Wu, Zhijiang Guo, Ngai Wong
- **Abstract**: While Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown significant potential in assisting peer review, current methods often struggle to generate thorough and insightful reviews while maintaining efficiency. In this paper, we propose TreeReview, a novel framework that models paper review as a hierarchical and bidirectional question-answering process. TreeReview first constructs a tree of review questions by recursively decomposing high-level questions into fine-grained sub-questions and then resolves the question tree by iteratively aggregating answers from leaf to root to get the final review. Crucially, we incorporate a dynamic question expansion mechanism to enable deeper probing by generating follow-up questions when needed. We construct a benchmark derived from ICLR and NeurIPS venues to evaluate our method on full review generation and actionable feedback comments generation tasks. Experimental results of both LLM-based and human evaluation show that TreeReview outperforms strong baselines in providing comprehensive, in-depth, and expert-aligned review feedback, while reducing LLM token usage by up to 80% compared to computationally intensive approaches. Our code and benchmark dataset are available at https://github.com/YuanChang98/tree-review.
- **Summary**: Here is a concise summary and rigorous evaluation of the prompt:

**Summary:**

The prompt instructs an expert reviewer to evaluate a scientific paper's novelty and significance. It requires a summary, a critical assessment with a score from 1 to 10, and a detailed justification supporting the score. The prompt emphasizes rigor, well-reasoned arguments, and a thorough discussion of strengths and weaknesses.

**Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Objective:** The prompt clearly defines the task and the expected output format.
    *   **Emphasis on Rigor:** The prompt repeatedly emphasizes the need for a rigorous, critical, and well-reasoned evaluation.
    *   **Specific Scoring Criteria:** The prompt provides a clear scale (1-10) and anchoring descriptions (negligible vs. exceptional) to guide the scoring process.
    *   **Balanced Perspective:** The prompt explicitly requires a discussion of both strengths and weaknesses, ensuring a balanced evaluation.
    *   **Focus on Novelty and Significance:** Aligns with core values of research assessment.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Subjectivity of Novelty/Significance:** Novelty and significance are subjective, and while the prompt emphasizes well-reasoned arguments, it could benefit from further clarifying what constitutes "novel" or "significant" within the relevant research context. This may lead to variations in interpretation amongst reviewers.
    *   **Potential for Biases:** Although not explicit, implicit biases related to the reviewer's own research interests, expertise, or perspective might influence the evaluation. It might be beneficial to explicitly remind reviewers to acknowledge their biases.

**Overall:**

The prompt is strong, providing a clear and structured framework for evaluating the novelty and significance of a scientific paper. The emphasis on rigor, critical thinking, and balanced assessment should encourage thoughtful and insightful reviews. To strengthen the prompt, the definition of "novelty" or "significance" could be added.

### Evaluating LLMs Robustness in Less Resourced Languages with Proxy Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07645v1)
- **Authors**: Maciej Chrabąszcz, Katarzyna Lorenc, Karolina Seweryn
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated impressive capabilities across various natural language processing (NLP) tasks in recent years. However, their susceptibility to jailbreaks and perturbations necessitates additional evaluations. Many LLMs are multilingual, but safety-related training data contains mainly high-resource languages like English. This can leave them vulnerable to perturbations in low-resource languages such as Polish. We show how surprisingly strong attacks can be cheaply created by altering just a few characters and using a small proxy model for word importance calculation. We find that these character and word-level attacks drastically alter the predictions of different LLMs, suggesting a potential vulnerability that can be used to circumvent their internal safety mechanisms. We validate our attack construction methodology on Polish, a low-resource language, and find potential vulnerabilities of LLMs in this language. Additionally, we show how it can be extended to other languages. We release the created datasets and code for further research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Evaluating LLMs Robustness in Less Resourced Languages with Proxy Models":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the vulnerability of large language models (LLMs) in low-resource languages, specifically Polish, to simple adversarial attacks. It proposes a framework for generating perturbed datasets using proxy models and attribution methods to identify the most important words in a sentence. These important words are then perturbed using character-level and word-level techniques (e.g., OCR errors, typos, swapping characters) to create adversarial examples. The robustness of LLMs is then evaluated on these perturbed datasets by measuring the Attack Success Rate (ASR). The paper demonstrates that even simple character and word-level attacks can significantly alter the predictions of various LLMs, highlighting a potential vulnerability, and suggests areas requiring more attention during the training and alignment process.  The paper releases the created datasets and code.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using proxy models for generating adversarial examples is not entirely new, however, the specific application to evaluating LLM robustness in *low-resource languages* is significant. The character-level and word-level perturbation techniques are standard but are combined with importance-based perturbations. Focusing on language-specific perturbations (like diacritical marks errors in Polish) adds some novelty. Releasing the dataset is a positive contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses an important issue: the uneven distribution of safety training data across languages and the potential vulnerability of LLMs in low-resource settings. Demonstrating that simple attacks can be effective is significant, as it shows that LLMs trained primarily on high-resource languages may not be robust in other languages. This has practical implications for the deployment of LLMs in multilingual contexts. By addressing these vulnerabilities, the analysis of the work assists model developers in improving the robustness of their models.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined methodology, including the framework for generating perturbed datasets and the use of proxy models.
    *   Empirical evaluation on a low-resource language (Polish).
    *   Detailed description of perturbation techniques.
    *   The release of the datasets and code.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Proxy models may not perfectly represent the target LLMs, potentially leading to inaccurate word importance calculations.
    *   The perturbations are limited to character-level and word-level modifications, and do not explore more complex adversarial attacks.
    *   The performance metric (ASR) is simple and doesn't consider the semantic similarity between original and perturbed outputs, e.g. some sentences may still be correctly understood even if the classification is incorrect.
    *   The paper uses a limited number of LLMs (Bielik, Mistral, Llama) and attribution methods for evaluation, which affects the generalizability of the claims.

*   **Potential Impact:** The work highlights an important area for improvement in multilingual LLMs. It provides a useful framework for assessing and potentially mitigating vulnerabilities in low-resource languages. The release of the dataset and code could encourage further research in this area, as researchers may find them useful to evaluate the vulnerability of their own LLMs in Polish.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a worthwhile and practical contribution to the evaluation of LLM robustness, specifically in the context of low-resource languages. It brings attention to an area often overlooked and offers a concrete methodology that can be readily adapted. While the novelty of individual components might be incremental, the combination of proxy models, targeted perturbations, and a focus on a low-resource language offers a valuable step forward. The limitations, such as the relatively simple attacks and reliance on proxy models, prevent a higher score. However, the paper has the potential to influence future research on multilingual LLM robustness and improve the deployment of these models in real-world settings.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### The Universality Lens: Why Even Highly Over-Parametrized Models Learn Well
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07661v1)
- **Authors**: Meir Feder, Ruediger Urbanke, Yaniv Fogel
- **Abstract**: A fundamental question in modern machine learning is why large, over-parameterized models, such as deep neural networks and transformers, tend to generalize well, even when their number of parameters far exceeds the number of training samples. We investigate this phenomenon through the lens of information theory, grounded in universal learning theory. Specifically, we study a Bayesian mixture learner with log-loss and (almost) uniform prior over an expansive hypothesis class. Our key result shows that the learner's regret is not determined by the overall size of the hypothesis class, but rather by the cumulative probability of all models that are close, in Kullback-Leibler divergence distance, to the true data-generating process. We refer to this cumulative probability as the weight of the hypothesis. This leads to a natural notion of model simplicity: simple models are those with large weight and thus require fewer samples to generalize, while complex models have small weight and need more data. This perspective provides a rigorous and intuitive explanation for why over-parameterized models often avoid overfitting: the presence of simple hypotheses allows the posterior to concentrate on them when supported by the data. We further bridge theory and practice by recalling that stochastic gradient descent with Langevin dynamics samples from the correct posterior distribution, enabling our theoretical learner to be approximated using standard machine learning methods combined with ensemble learning. Our analysis yields non-uniform regret bounds and aligns with key practical concepts such as flat minima and model distillation. The results apply broadly across online, batch, and supervised learning settings, offering a unified and principled understanding of the generalization behavior of modern AI systems.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary, critical evaluation, and score for the paper "The Universality Lens: Why Even Highly Over-Parametrized Models Learn Well."

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the fundamental question of why over-parameterized models (e.g., deep neural networks, transformers) generalize well even when the number of parameters exceeds the number of training samples.  It investigates this through the lens of information theory, specifically universal learning theory. The authors study a Bayesian mixture learner with log-loss and a uniform (or almost uniform) prior over a large hypothesis class. Their main result demonstrates that the learner's regret is *not* determined by the overall size of the hypothesis class but rather by the cumulative probability (weight) of models "close" (in Kullback-Leibler divergence) to the true data-generating process. This leads to a notion of model simplicity: simple models have large weight and require fewer samples, while complex models have small weight and need more data. The paper connects this theoretical finding to practical concepts like flat minima, model distillation, and stochastic gradient descent (SGD) with Langevin dynamics, arguing that such methods effectively sample from the posterior distribution predicted by their theory. Finally, the paper claims the results apply to online, batch and supervised learning settings, thus providing a unified view to generalisation in modern AI systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of relating generalization to the weight of the hypothesis class is interesting, though it builds on existing concepts in universal learning and information-theoretic bounds. The explicit connection to flat minima and the weight of the "e-environment" of the true model is more novel and provides an intuitive explanation for the success of over-parameterized models. The link to SGD with Langevin dynamics is a useful connection between theory and practice. However, it has been previously observed and noted that the algorithm converges to the posterior. Overall, the connections to prior literature are well made, but it is fair to say the originality of the paper's conclusions are limited.

*   **Significance:** The paper provides a theoretically grounded explanation for a phenomenon that is widely observed in practice. This has the potential to influence the design of new architectures and training methods. The framework may enable a more principled understanding of model complexity and guide the selection of appropriate model sizes for different tasks. Furthermore, the emphasis on *weight* as a measure of simplicity offers a potentially powerful alternative to traditional measures like VC dimension or Rademacher complexity, which often fail to provide meaningful bounds for over-parameterized models. The paper has the potential to influence future research in understanding the generalization behavior of deep learning models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper provides a rigorous theoretical framework.
    *   It offers an intuitive explanation for a complex phenomenon.
    *   It connects theoretical findings to practical concepts and algorithms.
    *   It yields potentially useful insights for model design.
    *   The claim for unifying the different settings into one generalised framework is intriguing.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The assumptions (e.g., the realizable case) may limit the applicability of the results in practice.
    *   The bounds are still asymptotic and may not be tight enough to provide precise guidance in real-world scenarios.
    *   The experimental validation is relatively limited. Further systematic experiments on real-world data are needed to strengthen the empirical support.
    *   While providing intuition, the bound still uses KL divergences that aren't particularly easy to work with in practice.
    *   Some of the core conclusions are not entirely original and build heavily on previous work.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper's framework may be adopted by researchers working on generalization theory.
    *   The emphasis on weight as a measure of simplicity may inspire new regularization techniques.
    *   The connection to SGD with Langevin dynamics could lead to more efficient training algorithms.
    *   Can lead to a new line of inquiry in understanding model complexity.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 6. The paper provides a well-reasoned and theoretically sound explanation for the generalization abilities of over-parameterized models. Its key strength lies in unifying multiple theoretical frameworks to reach its conclusions. The paper makes strong connections between theoretical insights and practical methods used in machine learning. However, the assumptions made limit the applicability, and the lack of extensive experimental validation prevents the paper from having a more substantial impact. Additionally, the core conclusions of the paper do not offer much originality.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### ProSplat: Improved Feed-Forward 3D Gaussian Splatting for Wide-Baseline Sparse Views
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07670v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaohan Lu, Jiaye Fu, Jiaqi Zhang, Zetian Song, Chuanmin Jia, Siwei Ma
- **Abstract**: Feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has recently demonstrated promising results for novel view synthesis (NVS) from sparse input views, particularly under narrow-baseline conditions. However, its performance significantly degrades in wide-baseline scenarios due to limited texture details and geometric inconsistencies across views. To address these challenges, in this paper, we propose ProSplat, a two-stage feed-forward framework designed for high-fidelity rendering under wide-baseline conditions. The first stage involves generating 3D Gaussian primitives via a 3DGS generator. In the second stage, rendered views from these primitives are enhanced through an improvement model. Specifically, this improvement model is based on a one-step diffusion model, further optimized by our proposed Maximum Overlap Reference view Injection (MORI) and Distance-Weighted Epipolar Attention (DWEA). MORI supplements missing texture and color by strategically selecting a reference view with maximum viewpoint overlap, while DWEA enforces geometric consistency using epipolar constraints. Additionally, we introduce a divide-and-conquer training strategy that aligns data distributions between the two stages through joint optimization. We evaluate ProSplat on the RealEstate10K and DL3DV-10K datasets under wide-baseline settings. Experimental results demonstrate that ProSplat achieves an average improvement of 1 dB in PSNR compared to recent SOTA methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ProSplat: Improved Feed-Forward 3D Gaussian Splatting for Wide-Baseline Sparse Views":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes ProSplat, a two-stage feed-forward framework for novel view synthesis (NVS) from sparse, wide-baseline input views.  The first stage utilizes a 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) generator (specifically, DepthSplat) to create 3D primitives and render initial views. The second stage employs an improvement model based on a one-step diffusion model to enhance the rendered views.  Key components of the improvement model are Maximum Overlap Reference view Injection (MORI), which selects the most relevant input view as a reference, and Distance-Weighted Epipolar Attention (DWEA), which enforces geometric consistency using epipolar constraints in the latent space.  A divide-and-conquer training strategy aligns the distributions between the two stages.  The method is evaluated on RealEstate10K and DL3DV-10K datasets under wide-baseline settings, showing improvements over existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper introduces a combination of existing and novel components, but the combination is what drives the result.
    * The core idea of using a feed-forward 3DGS generator (DepthSplat) isn't novel *per se*, as DepthSplat already exists.  However, applying it specifically to a wide-baseline sparse view problem and then augmenting the output with a diffusion model is a valid contribution.
    * MORI is a relatively straightforward view selection strategy, yet its simplicity is part of its appeal and effectiveness. It has some overlap with view selection strategies from other papers, but the specific application within a two-stage 3DGS framework has merit.
    * DWEA represents the most technically interesting novel aspect of the paper.  Integrating epipolar geometry into the attention mechanism of a diffusion model is a good contribution for improving geometric consistency, an often overlooked aspect of novel view synthesis.
    * The divide-and-conquer training strategy is a useful addition.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a practical and significant problem: generating high-quality novel views from sparse, wide-baseline imagery. This is relevant for applications like VR/AR, robotics, and autonomous navigation. The improvements demonstrated over existing feed-forward 3DGS methods are substantial and indicate that ProSplat is a step forward. The ablations do a solid job highlighting the impact of the different architectural choices of the paper.
    * The experiments are thorough and the quantitative results are clearly presented. The qualitative results provide visual confirmation of the improvements.
    * A weakness is that it builds directly on existing methods (DepthSplat, SD-Turbo), limiting its long-term impact. If DepthSplat is superceeded by another algorithm, ProSplat will need to be adapted to remain competitive.
    * The one-step diffusion model approach (while faster) could be perceived as trading off some generative power for speed. The authors acknowledge this in the limitation section, pointing out that there could be 'flat or over-smoothed textures', which future work could address.
    * The practical benefits of faster rendering with high-quality views are significant. It will likely spur further research in combining explicit (3DGS) and implicit (diffusion) representations for NVS.

**Justification of Score:**

ProSplat represents a solid incremental improvement on existing feed-forward 3DGS techniques, particularly for wide-baseline scenarios. The introduction of DWEA demonstrates good technical merit, and MORI provides a simple, efficient way of incorporating reference view information. The results and comprehensive experiments support the paper's claims. However, the heavy reliance on existing foundational methods (DepthSplat, SD-Turbo) and potential tradeoffs with a single-step diffusion architecture prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking contribution.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### QUITE: A Query Rewrite System Beyond Rules with LLM Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07675v1)
- **Authors**: Yuyang Song, Hanxu Yan, Jiale Lao, Yibo Wang, Yufei Li, Yuanchun Zhou, Jianguo Wang, Mingjie Tang
- **Abstract**: Query rewrite transforms SQL queries into semantically equivalent forms that run more efficiently. Existing approaches mainly rely on predefined rewrite rules, but they handle a limited subset of queries and can cause performance regressions. This limitation stems from three challenges of rule-based query rewrite: (1) it is hard to discover and verify new rules, (2) fixed rewrite rules do not generalize to new query patterns, and (3) some rewrite techniques cannot be expressed as fixed rules. Motivated by the fact that human experts exhibit significantly better rewrite ability but suffer from scalability, and Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated nearly human-level semantic and reasoning abilities, we propose a new approach of using LLMs to rewrite SQL queries beyond rules. Due to the hallucination problems in LLMs, directly applying LLMs often leads to nonequivalent and suboptimal queries. To address this issue, we propose QUITE (query rewrite), a training-free and feedback-aware system based on LLM agents that rewrites SQL queries into semantically equivalent forms with significantly better performance, covering a broader range of query patterns and rewrite strategies compared to rule-based methods. Firstly, we design a multi-agent framework controlled by a finite state machine (FSM) to equip LLMs with the ability to use external tools and enhance the rewrite process with real-time database feedback. Secondly, we develop a rewrite middleware to enhance the ability of LLMs to generate optimized query equivalents. Finally, we employ a novel hint injection technique to improve execution plans for rewritten queries. Extensive experiments show that QUITE reduces query execution time by up to 35.8% over state-of-the-art approaches and produces 24.1% more rewrites than prior methods, covering query cases that earlier systems did not handle.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces QUITE, a novel query rewrite system that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to improve SQL query performance. Unlike traditional rule-based systems, QUITE utilizes a training-free and feedback-aware approach based on LLM agents. QUITE employs a multi-agent framework controlled by a finite state machine (FSM) to manage the rewrite process.  The system incorporates a rewrite middleware with a structured knowledge base, a hybrid SQL corrector, and an agent memory buffer.  Additionally, QUITE utilizes a novel hint injection technique to improve the execution plans of rewritten queries. Experiments show that QUITE outperforms state-of-the-art approaches in reducing query execution time and producing a broader range of rewrites.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The novelty of QUITE lies in its innovative combination of LLMs, multi-agent systems, and database feedback for query rewriting. While LLMs have been used in database contexts before, the specific architecture of QUITE, with its FSM-controlled agents and middleware components, presents a unique approach. The hint injection technique is also a novel contribution.  The use of LLMs to go beyond rule-based rewrites is a notable step.
* **Significance:** The significance of QUITE stems from its ability to overcome limitations of existing query rewrite systems.  The system's improved performance and coverage demonstrate the potential of LLMs to handle more complex query patterns and rewrite strategies.  The feedback-aware and training-free nature of QUITE also make it potentially more adaptable and easier to deploy compared to systems that require extensive training data or rule engineering. The improved coverage for corner case queries offers significant benefit in overall workload performance. The effort of incorporating database insights into the rewrite process is a meaningful addition to the ongoing discussion of LLM application in database management. The inclusion of hint injection to address plan selection issues is a good example of addressing LLM weaknesses through conventional techniques.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Performance:**  The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the superiority of QUITE in terms of query execution time and rewrite coverage.
    * **Design:**  The architecture of QUITE, particularly the multi-agent FSM and the rewrite middleware, is well-designed and addresses key challenges in using LLMs for query rewriting (e.g., hallucination, context limitations).
    * **Training-Free:** The training-free aspect of QUITE is a significant advantage, as it reduces the complexity and cost of deployment.
    * **Hint Injection:** Addressing the shortcomings in optimizer estimates with hints demonstrates practical awareness and helps in realizing the full potential of rewrites.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **LLM Dependency:**  The system's reliance on LLMs introduces a dependency on external services, potentially impacting cost, availability, and reproducibility. Though the paper addresses issues of hallucination through its multi-agent approach, this problem isn’t completely removed. LLM costs need to be carefully considered.
    * **Complexity:** The system is quite complex with its multi-agent design, FSM and multiple LLM components, potentially adding to deployment/maintenance overhead.
    * **Experimental Setup:** While the workloads are well-known, there is a level of control required to achieve high fidelity in LLM-based experimentation. Lack of detailed descriptions of cost and iteration limits, along with LLM-specific prompts, diminishes potential for further reproducibility.
    * **Generality:** The experimental section, while thorough for the cases under evaluation, offers limited discussion to specific architectures or cases where QUITE may underperform other approaches.

* **Potential Influence:** QUITE has the potential to influence the future direction of query rewrite systems by demonstrating the effectiveness of LLMs in this domain.  The paper's architecture and techniques could serve as a blueprint for other researchers and practitioners seeking to leverage LLMs for database optimization. More fundamentally, the paper highlights the potential for intelligent agents to improve query optimization, offering a potential bridge for traditional query optimization and AI.

**Justification for Score:**

Despite the LLM dependency and implementation complexity, QUITE presents a genuinely innovative and promising approach to query rewriting. The significant performance improvements and the novel architecture warrant a high score. However, the dependency on LLM's and the somewhat complex implementation bring the score down.

- **Score**: 8/10

### Training Superior Sparse Autoencoders for Instruct Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07691v1)
- **Authors**: Jiaming Li, Haoran Ye, Yukun Chen, Xinyue Li, Lei Zhang, Hamid Alinejad-Rokny, Jimmy Chih-Hsien Peng, Min Yang
- **Abstract**: As large language models (LLMs) grow in scale and capability, understanding their internal mechanisms becomes increasingly critical. Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) have emerged as a key tool in mechanistic interpretability, enabling the extraction of human-interpretable features from LLMs. However, existing SAE training methods are primarily designed for base models, resulting in reduced reconstruction quality and interpretability when applied to instruct models. To bridge this gap, we propose $\underline{\textbf{F}}$inetuning-$\underline{\textbf{a}}$ligned $\underline{\textbf{S}}$equential $\underline{\textbf{T}}$raining ($\textit{FAST}$), a novel training method specifically tailored for instruct models. $\textit{FAST}$ aligns the training process with the data distribution and activation patterns characteristic of instruct models, resulting in substantial improvements in both reconstruction and feature interpretability. On Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, $\textit{FAST}$ achieves a mean squared error of 0.6468 in token reconstruction, significantly outperforming baseline methods with errors of 5.1985 and 1.5096. In feature interpretability, $\textit{FAST}$ yields a higher proportion of high-quality features, for Llama3.2-3B-Instruct, $21.1\%$ scored in the top range, compared to $7.0\%$ and $10.2\%$ for $\textit{BT(P)}$ and $\textit{BT(F)}$. Surprisingly, we discover that intervening on the activations of special tokens via the SAEs leads to improvements in output quality, suggesting new opportunities for fine-grained control of model behavior. Code, data, and 240 trained SAEs are available at https://github.com/Geaming2002/FAST.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Finetuning-aligned Sequential Training (FAST), a novel method for training Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) on instruction-tuned Large Language Models (LLMs). Existing SAE training methods are designed for base models and perform poorly on instruct models due to semantic discontinuities arising from data concatenation in the traditional Block Training paradigm. FAST addresses this issue by processing each data instance independently, preserving semantic integrity and aligning the training with the fine-tuning objectives of instruct models. Experimental results on Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct and Llama3.2-3B-Instruct demonstrate that FAST significantly improves token reconstruction accuracy and feature interpretability compared to Block Training baselines. The authors also investigate the influence of special tokens on model outputs using the trained SAEs, providing insights into their roles and potential for fine-grained model control.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a clear methodological advance in SAE training. The idea of sequential training, aligning with the finetuning process and focusing on the semantic integrity of individual data instances, addresses a significant limitation in applying SAEs to instruction-tuned models. This is a valuable contribution as instruct models become increasingly prevalent. The idea is simple but effectively addresses a real problem.

*   **Significance:** Improving SAE performance for instruct models has significant implications for mechanistic interpretability. The enhanced token reconstruction accuracy and feature interpretability obtained with FAST enable a deeper understanding of the inner workings of these models. The discovered ability to improve output quality through intervention on special token activations presents exciting possibilities for model control and behavior modification, which could lead to new areas for more fine-grained control of LLMs.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper is well-written and clearly articulates the problem, proposed solution, and experimental results.
    *   The motivation for FAST is compelling and directly addresses the shortcomings of existing Block Training methods.
    *   The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, comparing FAST against strong baselines on multiple models and tasks.
    *   The results demonstrate statistically significant improvements in token reconstruction and feature interpretability.
    *   The investigation into special token influence offers novel insights and potential avenues for future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the paper highlights the impact of special tokens on model outputs, it could delve deeper into the specific types of features and their interactions that contribute to improved or degraded performance upon intervention.
    *   The method depends on having high quality instruction tuning datasets, which could be a limitation in some scenarios.
    *   Computational constraints limit the investigation to models with fewer than 8B parameters; scalability to larger models should be verified.
    *   The feature steering experiments could explore a wider range of scaling coefficients (alpha) to determine a more precise optimal range and more exhaustively evaluate steering.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has strong potential to influence the field of mechanistic interpretability. FAST could become the standard method for training SAEs on instruction-tuned models, leading to more accurate and interpretable feature extraction. The findings on special token influence could inspire new research directions in model control, safety, and alignment. The open-sourcing of the code, datasets, and pre-trained SAE models will further promote adoption and experimentation.

*   **Rigorous Rationale for Score:** The method addresses a clear need in the growing field of LLM interpretability. The experiments demonstrate convincing improvements over baseline methods. The insights regarding special tokens are intriguing and point to valuable avenues for further investigation. While the approach is not revolutionary, it represents a significant practical advance in SAE training. It does have some of the limitations mentioned, but it improves current method.

### NOVA3D: Normal Aligned Video Diffusion Model for Single Image to 3D Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07698v1)
- **Authors**: Yuxiao Yang, Peihao Li, Yuhong Zhang, Junzhe Lu, Xianglong He, Minghan Qin, Weitao Wang, Haoqian Wang
- **Abstract**: 3D AI-generated content (AIGC) has made it increasingly accessible for anyone to become a 3D content creator. While recent methods leverage Score Distillation Sampling to distill 3D objects from pretrained image diffusion models, they often suffer from inadequate 3D priors, leading to insufficient multi-view consistency. In this work, we introduce NOVA3D, an innovative single-image-to-3D generation framework. Our key insight lies in leveraging strong 3D priors from a pretrained video diffusion model and integrating geometric information during multi-view video fine-tuning. To facilitate information exchange between color and geometric domains, we propose the Geometry-Temporal Alignment (GTA) attention mechanism, thereby improving generalization and multi-view consistency. Moreover, we introduce the de-conflict geometry fusion algorithm, which improves texture fidelity by addressing multi-view inaccuracies and resolving discrepancies in pose alignment. Extensive experiments validate the superiority of NOVA3D over existing baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "NOVA3D: Normal Aligned Video Diffusion Model for Single Image to 3D Generation" introduces a new approach for generating 3D objects from single images.  It leverages the 3D priors embedded in pre-trained video diffusion models by incorporating geometric information as auxiliary supervision. A key component is the Geometry-Temporal Alignment (GTA) attention mechanism, designed to facilitate information exchange between color and geometric domains within a Latent Video Diffusion Model (LVDM).  The method also features a de-conflict geometry fusion algorithm to improve texture fidelity by addressing multi-view inconsistencies and pose alignment issues.  Experiments on Google Scanned Objects and out-of-distribution images demonstrate the method's effectiveness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the specific combination of techniques and the architecture designed to exploit video diffusion models for single-image 3D generation. The core ideas, such as using diffusion models, distilling from pre-trained models, and incorporating geometric information, have been explored in prior work. However, the GTA attention mechanism to align RGB and normal map generation within a video diffusion model and the de-conflict geometry fusion algorithm seem to be unique contributions.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem: generating high-quality 3D models from single images, which is essential for many applications. The use of video diffusion models to capture 3D priors is a potentially impactful direction. The experimental results appear promising, showing improvements over existing baselines in terms of both geometry and texture quality.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Leveraging Video Diffusion Models:**  The paper effectively utilizes the inherent 3D understanding captured by video diffusion models, which are trained on sequences showing object rotations and viewpoint changes.
    * **GTA Attention Mechanism:**  The GTA mechanism is a well-motivated component designed to align texture and geometry information, addressing a crucial limitation in previous approaches.
    * **De-conflict Geometry Fusion:** The de-conflicting approach is a practical solution to improve the robustness and quality of the reconstructed mesh by handling inconsistencies.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough evaluation with quantitative and qualitative results across various datasets, including out-of-distribution examples.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Innovation:** While the approach combines existing techniques in a novel way, the individual components are not necessarily groundbreaking. The novelty hinges on how well these techniques integrate to provide a better 3D reconstruction.
    * **Dependency on Pre-trained Models:**  The method's performance relies heavily on the quality of the pre-trained video diffusion model.  Improvements in video diffusion models would directly benefit NOVA3D, but the approach itself may not advance the underlying video diffusion technology.
    * **Limited Ablation:**  While the ablation studies explore different model configurations, it would be helpful to see ablation of conflict modelling, pose refinement and so on. It is difficult to isolate the specific impact of individual components.
    * **Complexity:**  The method incorporates multiple modules, increasing complexity compared to simpler SDS-based approaches. The design choices made in the GTA attention mechanism and de-conflict fusion could be explained better, particularly the choice of specific architectural elements.

* **Potential Influence:**  If NOVA3D proves to be robust and generalizable, it could influence future research on single-image 3D generation. The idea of using video diffusion models and aligning texture and geometry information is a promising direction.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of single-image 3D generation. It effectively leverages video diffusion models and proposes innovative components to address key limitations of existing methods. While the individual components may not be entirely novel, their combination and the resulting performance are significant. The comprehensive evaluation supports the effectiveness of the approach. However, the incremental nature of the innovation and dependency on pre-trained models limit the overall impact. Taking into account all the pros and cons, a score of 7 reflects this balance.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Evaluating Robustness in Latent Diffusion Models via Embedding Level Augmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07706v1)
- **Authors**: Boris Martirosyan, Alexey Karmanov
- **Abstract**: Latent diffusion models (LDMs) achieve state-of-the-art performance across various tasks, including image generation and video synthesis. However, they generally lack robustness, a limitation that remains not fully explored in current research. In this paper, we propose several methods to address this gap. First, we hypothesize that the robustness of LDMs primarily should be measured without their text encoder, because if we take and explore the whole architecture, the problems of image generator and text encoders wll be fused. Second, we introduce novel data augmentation techniques designed to reveal robustness shortcomings in LDMs when processing diverse textual prompts. We then fine-tune Stable Diffusion 3 and Stable Diffusion XL models using Dreambooth, incorporating these proposed augmentation methods across multiple tasks. Finally, we propose a novel evaluation pipeline specifically tailored to assess the robustness of LDMs fine-tuned via Dreambooth.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the provided research paper, focusing on its contribution to robustness evaluation in Latent Diffusion Models (LDMs). Here's a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the lack of robustness in LDMs, which, despite achieving state-of-the-art performance in image generation, exhibit high sensitivity to textual prompts. The authors propose Augmentation of Embeddings with Latent Implicit Filtering (AELIF), a method that introduces data augmentation techniques directly to the text embeddings *after* the text encoder but *before* the denoising process in the LDM. AELIF involves two main components: embedding masking (replacing token embeddings with zero vectors) and embedding convolution (applying Gaussian noise to embeddings). The paper applies AELIF during the DreamBooth fine-tuning of Stable Diffusion 3 and Stable Diffusion XL models. Finally, the authors propose a tailored evaluation pipeline to assess the robustness of the fine-tuned LDMs, evaluating both overall image quality and robustness relative to the original training data. The experiments demonstrate improved robustness with AELIF while preserving image fidelity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies in the application of data augmentation at the *embedding level* specifically within LDMs, *after* the text encoder has done its job but *before* the denoising component. The specific techniques of embedding masking and noise convolution are also novel in this context, though the individual techniques themselves aren't entirely original. Additionally, the development of an evaluation pipeline specifically designed to measure robustness of the LDMs after fine-tuning with Dreambooth is also novel.
*   **Significance:** The paper tackles an important limitation of LDMs: their sensitivity to prompt variations. Addressing this robustness gap is crucial for deploying LDMs in real-world applications where prompts are often imperfect. The approach focuses on the LDM's denoising component itself, rather than text encoder issues, which is a valuable distinction. The potential for improved robustness *without* sacrificing fidelity is a significant contribution. However, the extent of this significance is limited by the specific models used (SD3 and SDXL) and the DreamBooth fine-tuning approach.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The method specifically targets the LDM's generative robustness, separate from text encoder issues.
    *   AELIF is relatively straightforward to implement.
    *   The evaluation pipeline is tailored to assess robustness after DreamBooth, which is important.
    *   The paper is well-structured and presents a clear methodology.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The experiments are limited to Stable Diffusion 3 and SDXL models fine-tuned via DreamBooth. It's unclear how well AELIF would generalize to other LDM architectures or fine-tuning techniques.
    *   While AELIF improves robustness it does not necessarily solve the issues. Figures 2 and 3 illustrate the problems with the original prompts.
    *   The reliance on CLIP embeddings in the evaluation pipeline introduces a potential bias, and while the Wasserstein distance is a reasonable metric, other measures might provide a more complete picture.
    *   The paper doesn't provide a detailed analysis of *why* AELIF works. What aspects of the LDM are most affected, and how do these augmentations influence the denoising process? This understanding would strengthen the paper's contribution.
    *   The novelty of masking and noise convolution is incremental. These techniques are borrowed from other areas.

*   **Potential Impact:** If AELIF proves effective across a wider range of LDMs and tasks, it could influence how these models are trained and deployed in production environments. The evaluation pipeline offers a solid framework for future research on robustness in LDMs.

**Justification for the Score:**

Given the paper's novel application of embedding-level augmentations to address a significant issue in LDMs, and the tailored evaluation pipeline, but acknowledging the limitations in generalizability, depth of analysis, and the incremental novelty of the specific augmentation techniques, I assign it a score of 6.

Score: 6
