### Interaction Analysis by Humans and AI: A Comparative Perspective
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07707v1)
- **Authors**: Maryam Teimouri, Filip Ginter, Tomi "bgt" Suovuo
- **Abstract**: This paper explores how Mixed Reality (MR) and 2D video conferencing influence children's communication during a gesture-based guessing game. Finnish-speaking participants engaged in a short collaborative task using two different setups: Microsoft HoloLens MR and Zoom. Audio-video recordings were transcribed and analyzed using Large Language Models (LLMs), enabling iterative correction, translation, and annotation. Despite limitations in annotations' accuracy and agreement, automated approaches significantly reduced processing time and allowed non-Finnish-speaking researchers to participate in data analysis. Evaluations highlight both the efficiency and constraints of LLM-based analyses for capturing children's interactions across these platforms. Initial findings indicate that MR fosters richer interaction, evidenced by higher emotional expression during annotation, and heightened engagement, while Zoom offers simplicity and accessibility. This study underscores the potential of MR to enhance collaborative learning experiences for children in distributed settings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the impact of Mixed Reality (MR) and traditional 2D video conferencing (Zoom) on children's communication during a collaborative gesture-based guessing game. It uses Large Language Models (LLMs) to automate the process of transcription, correction, translation, speaker identification, and emotion detection from audio-video recordings. The study compares a HoloLens-based MR setup with a Zoom-based setup, analyzing gameplay sessions and post-experiment interviews. The results suggest that MR fosters richer emotional expression and engagement, but Zoom offers simplicity and accessibility. The study also evaluates the effectiveness of LLMs in various NLP tasks related to children’s speech, highlighting both their efficiency and limitations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper combines two relatively well-established areas – MR/VR in education and the use of LLMs for text/speech processing. While neither of these areas is particularly novel in itself, the paper's originality lies in applying LLMs to analyze children's interactions in an MR environment, comparing it to traditional video conferencing, and the focus on Finnish data. Prior work might have looked at individual components, but the integrated approach, the particular use case, and the language-specific considerations add a layer of novelty.
    However, the application of LLMs for tasks like transcription and translation is becoming increasingly common. Therefore, the novelty is somewhat limited in terms of the *methodological approach*. The novelty is stronger with the *application of established methods to the given research question* around comparing MR and Zoom interaction with children and in understanding cross-lingual annotation.

*   **Significance:** The study contributes to the growing body of knowledge about the role of MR in education, specifically in promoting collaborative learning. The use of LLMs to streamline data processing and analysis makes it easier for researchers who are not native speakers to participate in data analysis. The comparison between MR and Zoom provides valuable insights for educators and designers who are considering using these technologies in the classroom.
    The findings suggest that MR could enhance engagement and emotional expression, but the technical challenges and potential for frustration need to be addressed. The detailed evaluation of LLM-based NLP tasks provides insights into their effectiveness and limitations in analyzing children’s speech. This is useful for researchers who are working on similar projects. A potential significant aspect is highlighting the role of MR for children's communication, especially given concerns surrounding social skills development with increased screen time; however, the study is initial and needs further replication. The paper offers some practical implications for educational settings in the current transition to virtual learning environments.
    However, the scope is limited by the small sample size and the focus on Finnish-speaking children. It also doesn’t provide robust evaluation metrics (just initial indicators) that would support claims of MR environments’ superiority for promoting engagement.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The study combines MR and LLMs to address an interesting research question.
    *   The use of LLMs reduces the time and cost of data processing and analysis.
    *   The comparative analysis between MR and Zoom provides valuable insights.
    *   The evaluation of LLMs provides useful information for researchers.
    *   Study provides detailed descriptions of the pipeline including all data used.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The sample size is small, limiting the generalizability of the results.
    *   The study focuses on Finnish-speaking children, which limits the generalizability of the results.
    *   The study doesn’t provide robust evaluation metrics to evaluate the quality of the interaction.
    *   The technical challenges and potential for frustration in MR environments are not fully addressed.
    *   A major missing piece is how data was validated at the end (error analysis).

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence researchers who are working on MR-based educational interventions or using LLMs for data analysis. It could also inform educators and designers who are considering using MR or Zoom in the classroom.
*   **Rigour:** The technical details of the NLP pipeline and the careful consideration of the evaluation metrics show an attention to detail. The paper could benefit from error analysis and more details on the data cleaning to assess robustness.

**Justification:**

The paper represents a solid contribution by integrating different research strands, even if its individual methodological components aren't groundbreaking. The application in a specific linguistic context and use case enhances its practicality and potential for direct application. The paper lacks some rigor and has important limitations in scale and generalizability. The main contributions are demonstrating feasibility of approach for a specialized domain.

### Through the Valley: Path to Effective Long CoT Training for Small Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07712v1)
- **Authors**: Renjie Luo, Jiaxi Li, Chen Huang, Wei Lu
- **Abstract**: Long chain-of-thought (CoT) supervision has become a common strategy to enhance reasoning in language models. While effective for large models, we identify a phenomenon we call Long CoT Degradation, in which small language models (SLMs; <=3B parameters) trained on limited long CoT data experience significant performance deterioration. Through extensive experiments on the Qwen2.5, LLaMA3 and Gemma3 families, we demonstrate that this degradation is widespread across SLMs. In some settings, models trained on only 8k long CoT examples lose up to 75% of their original performance before fine-tuning. Strikingly, we further observe that for some particularly small models, even training on 220k long CoT examples fails to recover or surpass their original performance prior to fine-tuning. Our analysis attributes this effect to error accumulation: while longer responses increase the capacity for multi-step reasoning, they also amplify the risk of compounding mistakes. Furthermore, we find that Long CoT Degradation may negatively impacts downstream reinforcement learning (RL), although this can be alleviated by sufficiently scaled supervised fine-tuning (SFT). Our findings challenge common assumptions about the benefits of long CoT training for SLMs and offer practical guidance for building more effective small-scale reasoning models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Through the Valley: Path to Effective Long CoT Training for Small Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the impact of long chain-of-thought (CoT) training on small language models (SLMs). The authors identify a phenomenon they term "Long CoT Degradation," where SLMs trained on limited long CoT data experience a significant drop in performance *before* fine-tuning. Through extensive experiments across various model families (Qwen, LLaMA, Gemma) and scales, they demonstrate the prevalence of this degradation. They attribute it to error accumulation due to the verbosity of long CoT responses and the SLMs' limited capacity to handle them. Furthermore, the paper explores the interplay between long CoT supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and subsequent reinforcement learning (RL), showing that while limited long CoT can hinder RL, sufficient long CoT data during SFT ultimately enhances RL performance. The study challenges the general assumption that long CoT is universally beneficial, especially for smaller models, and offers practical guidance for building effective reasoning models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in identifying and characterizing the "Long CoT Degradation" phenomenon in SLMs. While the benefits of CoT are well-established for large models, this work highlights its potential pitfalls when applied to models with limited capacity. The systematic investigation of degradation's prevalence across different architectures and the exploration of the interaction between SFT and RL specifically in the context of this degradation are valuable. The error accumulation analysis provides a potential causal mechanism for the observed effects. However, the underlying principles of error compounding and the limitations of SLMs in processing verbose text are somewhat understood, but the paper makes an effort to characterize it *specifically* for long CoT.

**Significance:** The findings have practical implications for researchers and practitioners working with SLMs for reasoning tasks. The paper cautions against blindly applying long CoT training without considering the model's capacity and the potential for performance degradation. The analysis offers guidelines on data scaling strategies and highlights the importance of sufficient long CoT SFT to unlock the benefits of RL. The identification of the degradation issue can also lead to developing specific mitigation strategies, such as length regularization or targeted training techniques to prevent SLMs from simply generating verbose outputs without actual improved reasoning. The work also underlines the need for careful hyperparameter optimization, data selection, and pre-training in the design of SLMs. This work will be crucial in the future development of SLMs.

**Strengths:**

*   **Thorough Empirical Study:** The paper presents a comprehensive set of experiments across multiple model families, scales, and benchmarks, providing strong evidence for the observed phenomenon.
*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The "Long CoT Degradation" is clearly defined and characterized.
*   **Mechanistic Analysis:** The attempt to identify the underlying cause (error accumulation) is a valuable step towards understanding the degradation.
*   **Practical Recommendations:**  The work offers practical guidance on data scaling and the effective integration of SFT and RL.
*   **Addresses a relevant and timely issue:** With the growing interest in deploying small models, understanding their limitations with respect to CoT is crucial.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The analysis focuses primarily on mathematical reasoning.  While the findings likely generalize to other domains involving multi-step reasoning, empirical validation across more diverse tasks would strengthen the conclusions.
*   **Simplicity of the error accumulation hypothesis:** While error accumulation makes sense intuitively, the analysis of it is not very rigurous and could be expanded upon. The synthetic benchmark does not fully capture the complexities of real world chain of thoughts.
*   **Lack of Mitigation Strategies:** While the paper identifies the problem, it doesn't propose or test specific methods to *mitigate* Long CoT Degradation (e.g., length regularization, targeted training, Curriculum SFT, etc.). Exploring such strategies would significantly enhance the paper's practical value.
*   **Limited Baseline and comparison:** The baselines used for RL training (i.e. no SFT) may not be the strongest possible baselines, as other works have found some success with short CoT SFT cold-starts. Also, there is not enough comparison or reference to other similar works.

**Score & Justification:**

I assign a score of **7**. The paper makes a solid contribution by identifying and characterizing a previously underexplored issue (Long CoT Degradation) in the context of SLMs. The systematic empirical analysis and mechanistic hypotheses are valuable.  However, the limitations, especially the narrow task domain and the lack of tested mitigation strategies, prevent it from achieving a higher score. The work opens up avenues for future research focused on developing effective techniques for leveraging long CoT to improve the reasoning abilities of SLMs without introducing unintended degradation. The paper challenges a common assumption in the field and offers insights that will be valuable to researchers working in this area.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Consistent Video Editing as Flow-Driven Image-to-Video Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07713v1)
- **Authors**: Ge Wang, Songlin Fan, Hangxu Liu, Quanjian Song, Hewei Wang, Jinfeng Xu
- **Abstract**: With the prosper of video diffusion models, down-stream applications like video editing have been significantly promoted without consuming much computational cost. One particular challenge in this task lies at the motion transfer process from the source video to the edited one, where it requires the consideration of the shape deformation in between, meanwhile maintaining the temporal consistency in the generated video sequence. However, existing methods fail to model complicated motion patterns for video editing, and are fundamentally limited to object replacement, where tasks with non-rigid object motions like multi-object and portrait editing are largely neglected. In this paper, we observe that optical flows offer a promising alternative in complex motion modeling, and present FlowV2V to re-investigate video editing as a task of flow-driven Image-to-Video (I2V) generation. Specifically, FlowV2V decomposes the entire pipeline into first-frame editing and conditional I2V generation, and simulates pseudo flow sequence that aligns with the deformed shape, thus ensuring the consistency during editing. Experimental results on DAVIS-EDIT with improvements of 13.67% and 50.66% on DOVER and warping error illustrate the superior temporal consistency and sample quality of FlowV2V compared to existing state-of-the-art ones. Furthermore, we conduct comprehensive ablation studies to analyze the internal functionalities of the first-frame paradigm and flow alignment in the proposed method.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FlowV2V, a novel approach for consistent video editing that leverages optical flow to guide an Image-to-Video (I2V) generation process. It addresses the challenge of maintaining temporal consistency and handling complex motion patterns, particularly non-rigid deformations, during video editing. FlowV2V decomposes the process into first-frame editing and conditional I2V generation, using shape-aligned pseudo flow sequences to ensure consistency. The method includes modules for First Frame Editing (FFE), Iterative Motion Propagation (IMP), Shape-Consistent Flow Calibration (SCFC), and Flow-Driven Image-to-Video Generation (FD-I2V). Experimental results on DAVIS-EDIT demonstrate improved temporal consistency and sample quality compared to existing methods. The paper also presents ablation studies analyzing the roles of different components.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addressing a Key Problem:** The paper tackles a significant challenge in video editing: consistent handling of non-rigid motion and shape deformation. Previous methods often struggle with such scenarios.
    *   **Novel Approach:** The use of optical flow as a primary driver for I2V generation, combined with shape alignment and calibration, is a relatively novel approach in the context of video editing using diffusion models.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough experimental evaluation using standard datasets (DAVIS-EDIT) and metrics (DOVER, FVD, CLIP scores). Ablation studies provide insights into the contributions of each module.
    *   **Qualitative Results:** The qualitative results demonstrate the ability of FlowV2V to handle challenging editing scenarios with non-rigid motions, which are superior than existing methods such as StableV2V and AnyV2V.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on I2V Backbones:** The method's performance is fundamentally limited by the capabilities of the underlying I2V generation model (SVD). This means that FlowV2V can only be as good as the base I2V model.
    *   **Computational Cost and Complexity:** While the paper claims that it has no extra computing resources, it is still a very complex process and requires significant computational resources.
    *   **Error Propagation:** As a multi-stage method, FlowV2V can be susceptible to error propagation. Errors in optical flow estimation (RAFT) or mask extraction can negatively impact the final result.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The evaluation focuses primarily on DAVIS-EDIT. While this is a standard dataset, evaluating on a more diverse set of video editing tasks would further strengthen the paper.

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using shape-aligned optical flow to guide I2V generation for video editing is novel. The iterative motion propagation and shape-consistent flow calibration modules contribute to the overall effectiveness of the approach.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to advance the state-of-the-art in video editing by enabling more consistent and realistic editing of videos with complex motion patterns. It could lead to improved tools for content creation, special effects, and video manipulation. The emphasis on addressing temporal consistency is particularly valuable. However, further improvements might be limited by the I2V backbone.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in video editing, particularly in the area of flow-guided I2V generation. It provides a strong foundation for developing more robust and versatile video editing tools.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to a significant problem in video editing. The use of optical flow for guiding I2V generation, along with shape alignment and calibration, represents a valuable contribution. The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the method compared to existing techniques.

However, the dependence on the I2V backbone and the potential for error propagation are limitations that need to be addressed. While the paper includes ablation studies, a more comprehensive analysis of the method's robustness to errors in optical flow estimation would be beneficial. Furthermore, the evaluation could be expanded to include a wider range of video editing tasks and datasets.

Overall, the paper makes a worthwhile contribution to the field of video editing, but its potential is somewhat constrained by its reliance on the underlying I2V model. Thus, a score of 7 reflects a solid but not exceptional contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### NeurIPS 2025 E2LM Competition : Early Training Evaluation of Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07731v1)
- **Authors**: Mouadh Yagoubi, Yasser Dahou, Billel Mokeddem, Younes Belkada, Phuc H. Le-Khac, Basma El Amel Boussaha, Reda Alami, Jingwei Zuo, Damiano Marsili, Mugariya Farooq, Mounia Lalmas, Georgia Gkioxari, Patrick Gallinari, Philip Torr, Hakim Hacid
- **Abstract**: Existing benchmarks have proven effective for assessing the performance of fully trained large language models. However, we find striking differences in the early training stages of small models, where benchmarks often fail to provide meaningful or discriminative signals. To explore how these differences arise, this competition tackles the challenge of designing scientific knowledge evaluation tasks specifically tailored for measuring early training progress of language models. Participants are invited to develop novel evaluation methodologies or adapt existing benchmarks to better capture performance differences among language models. To support this effort, we provide three pre-trained small models (0.5B, 1B, and 3B parameters), along with intermediate checkpoints sampled during training up to 200B tokens. All experiments and development work can be run on widely available free cloud-based GPU platforms, making participation accessible to researchers with limited computational resources. Submissions will be evaluated based on three criteria: the quality of the performance signal they produce, the consistency of model rankings at 1 trillion tokens of training, and their relevance to the scientific knowledge domain. By promoting the design of tailored evaluation strategies for early training, this competition aims to attract a broad range of participants from various disciplines, including those who may not be machine learning experts or have access to dedicated GPU resources. Ultimately, this initiative seeks to make foundational LLM research more systematic and benchmark-informed from the earliest phases of model development.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the NeurIPS 2025 E2LM Competition paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the "Early Training Evaluation of Language Models" (E2LM) competition at NeurIPS 2025. It addresses the issue that existing benchmarks for evaluating language models are often ineffective in the early training stages of small language models (SLMs), failing to provide meaningful or discriminative signals. The competition challenges participants to develop novel evaluation methodologies or adapt existing benchmarks specifically tailored to measure early training progress in SLMs, particularly regarding scientific knowledge acquisition. The authors provide pre-trained SLMs (0.5B, 1B, and 3B parameters) and intermediate checkpoints to support participant research. Submissions are evaluated based on signal quality, ranking consistency, and compliance with scientific knowledge domains. The goal is to make foundational LLM research more systematic and benchmark-informed from the earliest phases of model development.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of focusing on *early* training evaluation for *small* language models is where the relative novelty is positioned. Existing benchmarks are often designed for fully trained, large models, thus the paper correctly identifies a gap and proposes a solution. There have been challenges in the past focusing on reducing LLM size and edge device deployment, however, there is less attention to the initial phases of training. The approach of the competition by providing checkpoints and pre-trained models is a relatively standard mechanism. The suggested evaluation metrics -- signal quality, consistency, and domain compliance -- are reasonable, however, they don't represent a radical innovation. The idea of prompting and MMLU-var is a incremental modification.

*   **Significance:** The paper's potential significance lies in its ability to improve our understanding of how SLMs learn during early training, which could have implications for data mixture, hyperparameter tuning, and architectural choices. Better early training evaluations could accelerate the development of stronger SLMs in specialized domains (like scientific knowledge), and findings can be potentially transferred to larger LLM training. Making the competition accessible to researchers with limited computational resources helps democratize LLM research to a degree. If successful, this could lead to a more systematic and data-driven approach to training foundational LLMs, starting from the very beginning. The potential for cross-disciplinary collaboration is also a positive aspect.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear Problem Definition: The paper clearly articulates a real problem in the LLM evaluation landscape.
    *   Practical Focus: The competition structure and provision of resources make the research accessible and encourage practical solutions.
    *   Relevance: Focus on early training and knowledge acquisition addresses a crucial area of LLM research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Incremental Metrics: While reasonable, the evaluation metrics aren't groundbreaking; a truly novel contribution might have come from developing entirely new ways to quantify knowledge acquisition.
    *   Limited Scope of Innovation: The competition relies on adapting *existing* benchmarks; more emphasis on entirely new *types* of tasks could have been beneficial.
    *   Implementation Risk: Like all competitions, the impact depends heavily on participant engagement and the quality of the submissions.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    The paper's score lands at 7/10 because it identifies an important gap in LLM evaluation and proposes a well-structured solution. The lack of fundamental algorithmic innovation in the metrics design and scope is where the paper could have improved. The long-term significance depends on the outcome of the competition itself. If the competition prompts innovative new ways of evaluating SLMs early in their training, then this score may increase with time.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Language Embedding Meets Dynamic Graph: A New Exploration for Neural Architecture Representation Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07735v1)
- **Authors**: Haizhao Jing, Haokui Zhang, Zhenhao Shang, Rong Xiao, Peng Wang, Yanning Zhang
- **Abstract**: Neural Architecture Representation Learning aims to transform network models into feature representations for predicting network attributes, playing a crucial role in deploying and designing networks for real-world applications. Recently, inspired by the success of transformers, transformer-based models integrated with Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) have achieved significant progress in representation learning. However, current methods still have some limitations. First, existing methods overlook hardware attribute information, which conflicts with the current trend of diversified deep learning hardware and limits the practical applicability of models. Second, current encoding approaches rely on static adjacency matrices to represent topological structures, failing to capture the structural differences between computational nodes, which ultimately compromises encoding effectiveness. In this paper, we introduce LeDG-Former, an innovative framework that addresses these limitations through the synergistic integration of language-based semantic embedding and dynamic graph representation learning. Specifically, inspired by large language models (LLMs), we propose a language embedding framework where both neural architectures and hardware platform specifications are projected into a unified semantic space through tokenization and LLM processing, enabling zero-shot prediction across different hardware platforms for the first time. Then, we propose a dynamic graph-based transformer for modeling neural architectures, resulting in improved neural architecture modeling performance. On the NNLQP benchmark, LeDG-Former surpasses previous methods, establishing a new SOTA while demonstrating the first successful cross-hardware latency prediction capability. Furthermore, our framework achieves superior performance on the cell-structured NAS-Bench-101 and NAS-Bench-201 datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LeDG-Former, a novel framework for Neural Architecture Representation Learning. It addresses limitations in existing methods by incorporating hardware attribute information and using a dynamic graph-based transformer to model neural architectures.  LeDG-Former leverages language-based semantic embedding, inspired by Large Language Models (LLMs), to project both neural architectures and hardware platform specifications into a unified semantic space. This allows for zero-shot prediction across different hardware platforms. The dynamic graph-based transformer enhances the modeling of neural architectures by capturing structural differences between computational nodes. Experimental results on NNLQP, NAS-Bench-101, and NAS-Bench-201 datasets demonstrate LeDG-Former's superior performance compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents several novel aspects.

*   **Hardware-aware embedding:** Integrating hardware specifications into the neural architecture representation learning process is a significant step forward. Existing works largely ignore this crucial aspect, limiting their practical applicability.
*   **LLM-based embedding:** Utilizing LLMs to embed both neural architectures and hardware specifications into a unified semantic space enables cross-hardware zero-shot prediction.  This is a conceptually novel approach and addresses a real-world need.
*   **Dynamic Graph Self-Attention (DGSA):**  The proposed DGSA mechanism, which dynamically aggregates multi-scale topological information by retrieving relevant connectivity patterns, contributes towards a better representation of topological relationships across nodes.

**Significance:** The potential impact of this research is noteworthy.

*   **Improved Accuracy and Generalization:** The experimental results show that LeDG-Former surpasses previous methods on multiple datasets, indicating improved accuracy and generalization capabilities.
*   **Cross-Hardware Prediction:** The ability to perform zero-shot prediction across different hardware platforms is particularly valuable, as it allows for efficient model deployment and optimization in diverse environments.
*   **Better Representation Learning:** DGSA is shown through Ablation studies to improve existing methods.

**Strengths:**

*   The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing approaches.
*   The proposed LeDG-Former framework addresses these limitations with innovative techniques.
*   The experimental results are comprehensive and demonstrate the effectiveness of the framework.
*   The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component.

**Weaknesses:**

*   While the use of LLMs for embedding is promising, the paper does not delve deeply into the selection and fine-tuning of the LLM. The choice of BERT is mentioned, but a more detailed analysis of its suitability and potential alternatives would be beneficial.
*   The performance gains on NAS-Bench-101 and NAS-Bench-201 are marginal. The paper offers a reasonable explanation (simpler architectures), but further investigation into the applicability of LeDG-Former to more complex architectures is warranted.
*   Although the method achieves zero-shot prediction in a cross-hardware setting, it relies on fine-tuning on smaller dataset. A true zero-shot scenario would make the approach more impressive.
*   The hardware data covered in this study are fairly limited. Future work could evaluate this approach against more platforms or other edge devices.

**Overall Justification:**

The paper makes valuable contributions to the field of neural architecture representation learning. The integration of hardware information, the use of LLMs, and the dynamic graph-based transformer are innovative and address important limitations in existing methods. The improvements on the NNLQP benchmark and the demonstration of cross-hardware prediction capabilities are significant. However, the marginal gains on NAS-Bench-101 and NAS-Bench-201 and dependence on pre-training/fine-tuning prevent a higher score.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### RSafe: Incentivizing proactive reasoning to build robust and adaptive LLM safeguards
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07736v1)
- **Authors**: Jingnan Zheng, Xiangtian Ji, Yijun Lu, Chenhang Cui, Weixiang Zhao, Gelei Deng, Zhenkai Liang, An Zhang, Tat-Seng Chua
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) continue to exhibit vulnerabilities despite deliberate safety alignment efforts, posing significant risks to users and society. To safeguard against the risk of policy-violating content, system-level moderation via external guard models-designed to monitor LLM inputs and outputs and block potentially harmful content-has emerged as a prevalent mitigation strategy. Existing approaches of training guard models rely heavily on extensive human curated datasets and struggle with out-of-distribution threats, such as emerging harmful categories or jailbreak attacks. To address these limitations, we propose RSafe, an adaptive reasoning-based safeguard that conducts guided safety reasoning to provide robust protection within the scope of specified safety policies. RSafe operates in two stages: 1) guided reasoning, where it analyzes safety risks of input content through policy-guided step-by-step reasoning, and 2) reinforced alignment, where rule-based RL optimizes its reasoning paths to align with accurate safety prediction. This two-stage training paradigm enables RSafe to internalize safety principles to generalize safety protection capability over unseen or adversarial safety violation scenarios. During inference, RSafe accepts user-specified safety policies to provide enhanced safeguards tailored to specific safety requirements.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RSafe, an adaptive, reasoning-based safeguard for Large Language Models (LLMs). RSafe aims to address the limitations of traditional guard models, which rely heavily on human-curated datasets and struggle with out-of-distribution threats like emerging harmful categories and jailbreak attacks. RSafe operates in two stages: (1) *Guided Reasoning*, where it analyzes safety risks using policy-guided step-by-step reasoning, and (2) *Reinforced Alignment*, where rule-based reinforcement learning optimizes reasoning paths to align with accurate safety prediction.  During inference, RSafe can accept user-specified safety policies to provide enhanced safeguards tailored to specific needs. The authors demonstrate that RSafe matches state-of-the-art guard models with limited data and achieves superior out-of-distribution generalization, providing human-readable explanations for its safety judgments. The code is publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**

    * **Adaptive and Policy-Aware:**  The key strength of RSafe is its ability to adapt to user-specified safety policies. This is a significant improvement over existing guard models that are typically trained on a fixed taxonomy of harmful content and struggle when presented with new or custom safety requirements. The user-specified aspect enables robust performance across specific safety requirements.

    * **Reasoning-Based:** Incorporating explicit reasoning into the moderation process is a promising approach. It allows the model to generalize better to unseen safety violation scenarios by internalizing safety principles rather than simply memorizing patterns. The interpretability provided by the reasoning process is also valuable.

    * **Out-of-Distribution Generalization:** The experiments convincingly demonstrate RSafe's superior generalization capabilities against both emerging harmful categories and jailbreak attacks. This is crucial for deploying LLMs in dynamic, real-world environments.
    * **Data Efficiency:** RSafe achieves strong performance with a limited amount of training data, making it a more practical solution than methods that require extensive human annotation.

    * **Transparency and Interpretability:** RSafe offers detailed explanations for its safety judgments, increasing trust and facilitating debugging and improvement.

* **Weaknesses:**

    * **Reliance on Backbone LLM:** The performance of RSafe depends on the intrinsic reasoning capabilities of the underlying LLM. If the backbone model has limited reasoning abilities, RSafe's performance may be constrained. The dependency on the base LLM is a notable limitation of this framework.
    * **User Knowledge Requirement:** The effectiveness of custom safety policy input relies on the user's ability to define appropriate taxonomies for new or diverging contexts. If users lack this knowledge, RSafe's generalization capability may be limited.

    * **Limited Scenarios:** The paper does not deeply investigate scenarios where a combination of categories could be violated simultaneously, nor the reasoning complexity when many policies are specified. The experimental section covers only a subset of potential cases.
    * **Baselines Selection:** While several baselines are included, a deeper exploration of state-of-the-art compositional methods (methods able to combine multiple judgements) would add value to the novelty assessment.

* **Novelty and Significance:**

    The idea of incorporating reasoning into guard models is not entirely new (GuardReasoner is mentioned), but RSafe's specific approach of using rule-based RL to align reasoning paths with accurate safety prediction, combined with its focus on adaptive safety policies, represents a significant advancement.  The demonstrated out-of-distribution generalization capabilities are particularly important in a rapidly evolving threat landscape. The design is well-integrated, leveraging both strengths from other methods, and addressing the issues related to safety in modern LLMs.

* **Potential Impact:**

    RSafe has the potential to significantly improve the robustness and adaptability of LLM safety moderation systems.  Its ability to handle user-specified safety policies and generalize to unseen threats could make it a valuable tool for deploying LLMs in a wide range of applications.

**Justification for Score:**

RSafe's strengths in adaptive policy enforcement, reasoning-based moderation, and out-of-distribution generalization are significant. While the reliance on the backbone LLM and the user's knowledge of safety categories are limitations, the overall contribution is substantial. The code's availability also supports wider adoption. The baselines selected are representative, and the experimental validation is appropriate, even if some areas could be explored with greater depth. RSafe represents a solid step forward in creating more robust and adaptable LLM safeguards.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### ArchiLense: A Framework for Quantitative Analysis of Architectural Styles Based on Vision Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07739v1)
- **Authors**: Jing Zhong, Jun Yin, Peilin Li, Pengyu Zeng, Miao Zhang, Shuai Lu, Ran Luo
- **Abstract**: Architectural cultures across regions are characterized by stylistic diversity, shaped by historical, social, and technological contexts in addition to geograph-ical conditions. Understanding architectural styles requires the ability to describe and analyze the stylistic features of different architects from various regions through visual observations of architectural imagery. However, traditional studies of architectural culture have largely relied on subjective expert interpretations and historical literature reviews, often suffering from regional biases and limited ex-planatory scope. To address these challenges, this study proposes three core contributions: (1) We construct a professional architectural style dataset named ArchDiffBench, which comprises 1,765 high-quality architectural images and their corresponding style annotations, collected from different regions and historical periods. (2) We propose ArchiLense, an analytical framework grounded in Vision-Language Models and constructed using the ArchDiffBench dataset. By integrating ad-vanced computer vision techniques, deep learning, and machine learning algo-rithms, ArchiLense enables automatic recognition, comparison, and precise classi-fication of architectural imagery, producing descriptive language outputs that ar-ticulate stylistic differences. (3) Extensive evaluations show that ArchiLense achieves strong performance in architectural style recognition, with a 92.4% con-sistency rate with expert annotations and 84.5% classification accuracy, effec-tively capturing stylistic distinctions across images. The proposed approach transcends the subjectivity inherent in traditional analyses and offers a more objective and accurate perspective for comparative studies of architectural culture.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "ArchiLense," a framework for quantitatively analyzing architectural styles using vision large language models (VLLMs). It addresses the limitations of traditional, subjective approaches to architectural style analysis, which often suffer from regional biases and limited explanatory scope. The paper contributes a professional architectural style dataset called ArchDiffBench, and ArchiLense itself, which integrates computer vision, deep learning, and machine learning to automate the recognition, comparison, and classification of architectural imagery. The system generates descriptive language outputs that articulate stylistic differences. Evaluations demonstrate strong performance in architectural style recognition.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies in its application of VLLMs to the specific problem of architectural style analysis. While VLLMs are a relatively recent development, their application in architecture is less explored, especially for quantitative analysis rather than just image generation. The construction of the ArchDiffBench dataset also adds to the novelty, providing a dedicated resource for this specific task. The two stage approach to style extraction and style evaluation is also somewhat novel.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to provide a more objective and data-driven approach to architectural style analysis. This can be valuable for researchers, architects, and heritage professionals who need to understand stylistic differences across regions and historical periods. The ability to automatically generate descriptive language outputs is also a significant advancement.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a clear and relevant problem in the field.
    *   Introduces a novel framework with clear methodology.
    *   Constructs a specialized dataset (ArchDiffBench).
    *   Demonstrates strong performance through evaluations.
    *   Offers a more objective and accurate perspective than traditional methods.
    *   Provides a robust framework for more objective and accurate comparative studies of architectural culture.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Dataset bias:  The paper acknowledges a dataset bias toward modern Western styles, which limits the generalizability of the findings. This is a significant limitation, as it restricts the framework's applicability to a broader range of architectural styles and cultures.
    *   Spatial Authenticity: The paper acknowledges limited spatial authenticity.
    *   Limited Analysis of spatial authenticity: The analysis primarily focuses on visual style and textual descriptions and how they compare, but could greatly benefit from including spatial information (e.g. floorplans) as well.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more in-depth comparison with other AI-based architectural style analysis methods.
    *   Theoretical Depth: The paper acknowledges the need for more in-depth theoretical engagement to improve generalizability and interpretability across cultural contexts.
*   **Justification for Score:**

    While the application of VLLMs to architectural style analysis is innovative and the framework performs well, the limitations regarding dataset bias and limited spatial context impact the overall significance. Addressing these biases is critical for broader applicability. The paper takes a substantial step toward a more objective and automated analysis of architectural styles, but needs further refinement. The two-stage analysis system could also benefit from integration.

### Research quality evaluation by AI in the era of Large Language Models: Advantages, disadvantages, and systemic effects
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07748v1)
- **Authors**: Mike Thelwall
- **Abstract**: Artificial Intelligence (AI) technologies like ChatGPT now threaten bibliometrics as the primary generators of research quality indicators. They are already used in at least one research quality evaluation system and evidence suggests that they are used informally by many peer reviewers. Since using bibliometrics to support research evaluation continues to be controversial, this article reviews the corresponding advantages and disadvantages of AI-generated quality scores. From a technical perspective, generative AI based on Large Language Models (LLMs) equals or surpasses bibliometrics in most important dimensions, including accuracy (mostly higher correlations with human scores), and coverage (more fields, more recent years) and may reflect more research quality dimensions. Like bibliometrics, current LLMs do not "measure" research quality, however. On the clearly negative side, LLM biases are currently unknown for research evaluation, and LLM scores are less transparent than citation counts. From a systemic perspective, the key issue is how introducing LLM-based indicators into research evaluation will change the behaviour of researchers. Whilst bibliometrics encourage some authors to target journals with high impact factors or to try to write highly cited work, LLM-based indicators may push them towards writing misleading abstracts and overselling their work in the hope of impressing the AI. Moreover, if AI-generated journal indicators replace impact factors, then this would encourage journals to allow authors to oversell their work in abstracts, threatening the integrity of the academic record.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the potential of AI, specifically Large Language Models (LLMs) like ChatGPT, in research quality evaluation. It contrasts LLMs with traditional bibliometric methods and expert review, outlining the advantages (potentially higher accuracy, broader coverage, assessment of more quality dimensions) and disadvantages (unknown biases, lower transparency, gaming potential) of LLMs. It argues that while LLMs hold promise for research evaluation, there are too many unknowns to replace existing evaluation methods completely and that more research is needed to understand their biases and limitations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper itself isn't profoundly novel. The idea of using LLMs for research evaluation is gaining traction, and several papers have already investigated their correlation with human judgments, some of which this paper references. The novelty lies more in the comprehensive synthesis of the arguments, drawing together the technical capabilities, practical implications, and ethical considerations. It provides a valuable overview of the current state of the debate. However, it does not present any new experimental data or techniques for overcoming the limitations it outlines.
*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper rests on its timely discussion of a rapidly evolving field. As LLMs become more sophisticated and integrated into research workflows, understanding their potential impact on evaluation processes is crucial. The paper highlights the critical need for caution and further research before widespread adoption. The identification of potential gaming scenarios and the emphasis on ethical considerations are particularly important. The paper effectively highlights key potential risks and opportunities.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Balanced perspective: The paper presents a balanced view of LLMs, acknowledging both their potential benefits and drawbacks.
    *   Comprehensive scope: It covers technical, practical, and ethical aspects, offering a holistic overview.
    *   Clear articulation of key issues: The paper clearly articulates the critical questions that need to be addressed before LLMs can be responsibly used in research evaluation.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Lack of original empirical data: The paper relies primarily on existing literature. While it synthesizes this literature effectively, it does not offer new evidence to support its claims.
    *   Speculative nature: Some arguments, particularly regarding gaming potential and unforeseen biases, are somewhat speculative. Further empirical investigation is needed to validate these concerns.
    *   Limited discussion of solutions: While the paper identifies potential problems, it provides limited discussion of concrete solutions or mitigation strategies. How *can* these biases be understood and mitigated? How can gaming be prevented?

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **6**. This score reflects the paper's value as a timely and balanced overview of the use of LLMs in research evaluation and its clear articulation of critical issues. However, the lack of original empirical data and the speculative nature of some arguments limits its impact. While the paper is a useful contribution to the ongoing discussion, it does not represent a groundbreaking advancement in the field. The score is therefore "good" but not "exceptional". The primary limitation is the lack of novel investigation into solutions to the outlined issues.

### Difference Inversion: Interpolate and Isolate the Difference with Token Consistency for Image Analogy Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07750v1)
- **Authors**: Hyunsoo Kim, Donghyun Kim, Suhyun Kim
- **Abstract**: How can we generate an image B' that satisfies A:A'::B:B', given the input images A,A' and B? Recent works have tackled this challenge through approaches like visual in-context learning or visual instruction. However, these methods are typically limited to specific models (e.g. InstructPix2Pix. Inpainting models) rather than general diffusion models (e.g. Stable Diffusion, SDXL). This dependency may lead to inherited biases or lower editing capabilities. In this paper, we propose Difference Inversion, a method that isolates only the difference from A and A' and applies it to B to generate a plausible B'. To address model dependency, it is crucial to structure prompts in the form of a "Full Prompt" suitable for input to stable diffusion models, rather than using an "Instruction Prompt". To this end, we accurately extract the Difference between A and A' and combine it with the prompt of B, enabling a plug-and-play application of the difference. To extract a precise difference, we first identify it through 1) Delta Interpolation. Additionally, to ensure accurate training, we propose the 2) Token Consistency Loss and 3) Zero Initialization of Token Embeddings. Our extensive experiments demonstrate that Difference Inversion outperforms existing baselines both quantitatively and qualitatively, indicating its ability to generate more feasible B' in a model-agnostic manner.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the provided paper. Here's a summary and critical evaluation.

**Summary**

The paper introduces "Difference Inversion," a novel method for generating image analogies. Given an image triplet (A, A', B), the goal is to produce a target image B' such that A : A' :: B : B'. The core idea is to isolate and apply the *difference* between images A and A' to image B to generate B'.  The method avoids reliance on text prompts, unlike many existing conditional image generation techniques. Difference Inversion tackles the problem of model dependency and potential biases by encoding the difference between A and A' using "Difference Tokens" optimized through several novel techniques: Delta Interpolation (to define a more precise difference), Token Consistency Loss (to ensure that the difference appears and disappears appropriately), and Zero Initialization of Token Embeddings (to prevent bias).  The paper demonstrates, through quantitative and qualitative experiments, that Difference Inversion outperforms existing baselines and can be applied to various diffusion models in a model-agnostic way.

**Critical Evaluation**

**Novelty:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The core idea of isolating and directly encoding the *difference* between images using "Difference Tokens" is a significant and well-motivated departure from existing approaches. Previous methods rely heavily on text prompts or are tied to specific diffusion models or inpainting approaches.
    *   The introduction of Delta Interpolation, Token Consistency Loss, and Zero Initialization of Token Embeddings are novel techniques tailored to the specific challenges of isolating and encoding image differences.
    *   The model-agnostic nature of the method is a valuable contribution, allowing it to be used with various diffusion models (e.g., Stable Diffusion, SDXL).
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The inspiration is inspired by VISII. The key idea of training tokens to represent the difference has already been proposed in VISII.
    *   While the overall approach is novel, the reliance on pre-trained models and existing techniques (like PEZ for prompt optimization, CLIP, and DDIM inversion) somewhat diminishes the fundamental novelty. They are combining these existing techniques to achieve a novel result.
    *   The paper acknowledges the limitations of the CLIP space and the challenges in accurately capturing complex or subtle differences.

**Significance:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The ability to generate image analogies *without* relying on text prompts is a substantial advantage, making the method more flexible and broadly applicable.
    *   The improved performance (both quantitative and qualitative) compared to existing baselines demonstrates the effectiveness of the approach.
    *   The model-agnostic nature increases the impact and usability of the work, allowing other researchers and practitioners to readily adopt it.
    *   The work opens up new avenues for image editing and manipulation by explicitly focusing on and controlling the differences between images.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The acknowledged limitations regarding complex or subtle differences highlight areas where the method may not be applicable.
    *   While the experiments are thorough, further investigation into the method's performance on a wider range of image types and transformations would strengthen the findings.
    *   The paper doesn't deeply explore potential applications of Difference Inversion beyond image analogy generation.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to image analogy generation.  The core idea of isolating and encoding image differences is significant and addresses limitations of existing methods. The techniques introduced (Delta Interpolation, etc.) are well-motivated and contribute to the effectiveness of the approach. The model-agnostic nature increases the impact and usability of the work.

However, the reliance on existing pre-trained models and existing techniques diminishes the fundamental novelty. In addition, the model still has some shortcomings.

The presented findings and insights are valuable to the image generation and editing communities.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Augmenting LLMs' Reasoning by Reinforcing Abstract Thinking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07751v1)
- **Authors**: Silin Gao, Antoine Bosselut, Samy Bengio, Emmanuel Abbe
- **Abstract**: Recent studies have shown that large language models (LLMs), especially smaller ones, often lack robustness in their reasoning. I.e., they tend to experience performance drops when faced with distribution shifts, such as changes to numerical or nominal variables, or insertions of distracting clauses. A possible strategy to address this involves generating synthetic data to further "instantiate" reasoning problems on potential variations. In contrast, our approach focuses on "abstracting" reasoning problems. This not only helps counteract distribution shifts but also facilitates the connection to symbolic tools for deriving solutions. We find that this abstraction process is better acquired through reinforcement learning (RL) than just supervised fine-tuning, which often fails to produce faithful abstractions. Our method, AbstraL -- which promotes abstract reasoning in LLMs using RL on granular abstraction data -- significantly mitigates performance degradation on recent GSM perturbation benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Augmenting LLMs' Reasoning by Reinforcing Abstract Thinking" addresses the challenge of robustness in large language model (LLM) reasoning, particularly in smaller models, when faced with distribution shifts (e.g., changes in numerical variables, distracting clauses).  Instead of generating more varied training instances, the authors propose a novel approach called AbstraL, which teaches LLMs to explicitly construct underlying abstractions of reasoning problems. This involves a reinforced learning (RL) framework leveraging "Granularly-decomposed Abstract Reasoning (GranulAR)" data distilled from an oracle LLM. The process consists of identifying conditions, abstract reasoning, abstraction retrieval, and symbolic derivation.  Experiments on GSM-Symbolic and GSM-Plus benchmarks demonstrate that AbstraL mitigates performance degradation caused by perturbations and distractions, improving reasoning robustness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of focusing on *abstracting* reasoning problems rather than *instantiating* more varied examples is a significant and novel contribution. Many prior approaches have focused on data augmentation.  The RL-based learning framework to *directly* teach LLMs to abstract is also novel. The GranulAR data generation methodology, leveraging an oracle model and decomposing the process into finer steps, adds another layer of novelty. The introduction of model-free rewards tied directly to abstraction quality further distinguishes this work.

*   **Significance:** The problem of robustness in LLM reasoning is crucial for real-world deployment, especially in applications where models encounter diverse and unexpected inputs. This research offers a promising strategy to improve generalization capabilities. The practical implications of reducing reliance on massive data augmentation are considerable, as it could lead to more efficient and accessible training pipelines.  The improvement in handling distracting information is also significant, as it addresses a common failure point for LLMs.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the robustness challenge and its impact on LLM reasoning.
    *   **Novel Approach:** The AbstraL framework presents a fundamentally different approach to improving robustness compared to existing data augmentation techniques.
    *   **Granular Decomposition:** The GranulAR data format seems very effective at decomposing the overall problem, and providing a curriculum to ease learning in LLMs.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides compelling empirical evidence on two established benchmarks (GSM-Symbolic and GSM-Plus), showcasing the effectiveness of AbstraL across various LLM sizes.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies are critical for isolating the contributions of different components of the framework (e.g., RL, symbolic tools, contextual information), strengthening the claims made.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on an Oracle LLM for Data Generation:** The GranulAR data creation process depends on a powerful oracle LLM, which might limit the accessibility of the method. While the trained LLM demonstrates improved robustness, the initial data distillation requires a significant resource. However, authors have demonstrated that the weaker LLM may be fine-tuned through RL, thus mitigating initial resource costs.
    *   **Limited Scope of Evaluation:**  The evaluation focuses primarily on mathematical reasoning. While this is a representative domain, it would be valuable to see how well AbstraL generalizes to other reasoning tasks (e.g., commonsense reasoning, logical inference).
    *   **Complexity:** The AbstraL framework is relatively complex, involving multiple stages and components. This might present a barrier to adoption. However, the authors thoroughly describe and provide results of the ablation, which mitigates its complexity.
    *   **Greedy Decoding:** The authors have tested their model using greedy decoding only. Advanced decoding strategies have not been tested, such as self-consistency decoding.

*   **Potential Influence:**  This paper has the potential to significantly influence research on LLM reasoning, robustness, and abstract thinking.  It provides a concrete and effective method for improving generalization, and the underlying principles could be adapted to other tasks and domains.  It also highlights the importance of directly learning abstract representations, which could inspire new research directions.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper presents a novel and well-validated approach to a crucial problem in LLM research. The experimental results are compelling, and the ablation studies provide valuable insights. While the reliance on an oracle LLM and limited scope of evaluation are drawbacks, the overall contribution is significant and likely to have a lasting impact.

- **Score**: 8/10

### REMoH: A Reflective Evolution of Multi-objective Heuristics approach via Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07759v1)
- **Authors**: Diego Forniés-Tabuenca, Alejandro Uribe, Urtzi Otamendi, Arkaitz Artetxe, Juan Carlos Rivera, Oier Lopez de Lacalle
- **Abstract**: Multi-objective optimization is fundamental in complex decision-making tasks. Traditional algorithms, while effective, often demand extensive problem-specific modeling and struggle to adapt to nonlinear structures. Recent advances in Large Language Models (LLMs) offer enhanced explainability, adaptability, and reasoning. This work proposes Reflective Evolution of Multi-objective Heuristics (REMoH), a novel framework integrating NSGA-II with LLM-based heuristic generation. A key innovation is a reflection mechanism that uses clustering and search-space reflection to guide the creation of diverse, high-quality heuristics, improving convergence and maintaining solution diversity. The approach is evaluated on the Flexible Job Shop Scheduling Problem (FJSSP) in-depth benchmarking against state-of-the-art methods using three instance datasets: Dauzere, Barnes, and Brandimarte. Results demonstrate that REMoH achieves competitive results compared to state-of-the-art approaches with reduced modeling effort and enhanced adaptability. These findings underscore the potential of LLMs to augment traditional optimization, offering greater flexibility, interpretability, and robustness in multi-objective scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces REMOH, a novel framework for multi-objective optimization that integrates Large Language Models (LLMs) with the NSGA-II algorithm. The key innovation is a "reflection mechanism" that leverages clustering and search-space reflection to guide the LLM in generating diverse, high-quality heuristics.  The approach is evaluated on the Flexible Job Shop Scheduling Problem (FJSSP) using standard datasets and compared to state-of-the-art methods, including mathematical programming and reinforcement learning techniques. The results suggest that REMOH achieves competitive performance with reduced problem-specific modeling effort and enhanced adaptability compared to traditional methods. The authors also highlight the framework's flexibility in incorporating complex constraints.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using an LLM within an evolutionary framework for heuristic generation is not entirely new (as the paper itself acknowledges). However, the specific implementation of the reflection mechanism, which actively guides the LLM's search based on performance analysis of the heuristic population, provides a potentially significant contribution. The hierarchical approach, involving both short and long reflections, seems carefully designed.

*   **Significance:** The paper's potential significance lies in its attempt to bridge the gap between traditional optimization techniques and the reasoning and generative power of LLMs.  If REMOH can genuinely reduce the need for extensive problem-specific modeling while maintaining or improving performance, it could have a tangible impact. The FJSSP is a good test case, as it is a well-studied but still challenging problem.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The reflection mechanism offers a structured way to incorporate LLM knowledge and reasoning into the evolutionary process.
    *   The framework demonstrates strong modeling flexibility by the integration of context-sensitive constraints with little effort.
    *   The experimental evaluation is relatively comprehensive, with benchmarking against several established methods on standard datasets.
    *   The authors perform an ablation study to demonstrate the importance of the reflection mechanism, which strengthens the claims.
    * The paper is well-written and clearly explains the REMOH framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The "competitive results" need further scrutiny. While the paper presents GAP values and comparisons, it's crucial to consider whether the improvements over traditional methods are statistically significant and practically meaningful in real-world scenarios. The analysis could benefit from more in-depth statistical testing.

    *   The reliance on specific LLMs (Gemini in the final experiments) raises concerns about reproducibility and generalizability. LLMs are constantly evolving, and the performance of REMOH might be highly dependent on the specific LLM used.  The prompt engineering aspects also need to be thoroughly documented to enable independent replication.

    *   The computational overhead is mentioned. A more detailed analysis and comparison of the computational cost of REMOH compared to traditional methods would be useful.

    *   While the paper highlights the adaptability of REMOH, the evaluation is limited to FJSSP. Demonstrating the approach's effectiveness on other multi-objective optimization problems would strengthen this claim. Also, while the modeling flexibility is a plus, the prompt engineering still requires considerable expertise, which should be acknowledged.

    * It's not clear if the generated heuristics are human-competitive. Demonstrating this would significantly strengthen the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the strengths of REMOH can be further validated and its limitations addressed, it could influence future research in automated algorithm design and optimization, especially in scenarios where problem-specific expertise is scarce or rapidly changing. It might also contribute to the development of more adaptable and interpretable optimization methods.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper presents a genuinely interesting integration of LLMs and evolutionary algorithms. However, the contributions, while innovative, are somewhat incremental relative to the existing literature in the field of automated algorithm design. The reliance on LLMs as black boxes, combined with the potential for performance variation across different LLMs, limits the generalizability and interpretability of the results. Also, the current results need stronger statistical analysis. REMOH is a promising starting point, but further work is required to demonstrate its robustness and superiority.

### Diffusion Models-Aided Uplink Channel Estimation for RIS-Assisted Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07770v1)
- **Authors**: Yang Wang, Yin Xu, Cixiao Zhang, Zhiyong Chen, Xiaowu Ou, Mingzeng Dai, Meixia Tao, Wenjun Zhang
- **Abstract**: This letter proposes a channel estimation method for reconfigurable intelligent surface (RIS)-assisted systems through a novel diffusion model (DM) framework. We reformulate the channel estimation problem as a denoising process, which aligns with the reverse process of the DM. To overcome the inherent randomness in the reverse process of conventional DM approaches, we adopt a deterministic sampling strategy with a step alignment mechanism that ensures the accuracy of channel estimation while adapting to different signal-to-noise ratio (SNR). Furthermore, to reduce the number of parameters of the U-Net, we meticulously design a lightweight network that achieves comparable performance, thereby enhancing the practicality of our proposed method. Extensive simulations demonstrate superior performance over a wide range of SNRs compared to baselines. For instance, the proposed method achieves performance improvements of up to 13.5 dB in normalized mean square error (NMSE) at SNR = 0 dB. Notably, the proposed lightweight network exhibits almost no performance loss compared to the original U-Net, while requiring only 6.59\% of its parameters.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a diffusion model (DM)-based channel estimation method for RIS-assisted wireless systems. It reframes the channel estimation problem as a denoising process, aligning with the DM's reverse process. To improve accuracy and adaptability to different SNR levels, the authors introduce a deterministic sampling strategy with a step alignment mechanism. They also present a lightweight U-Net architecture (BRCNet) to reduce model complexity while maintaining performance. Simulations demonstrate that the proposed method outperforms traditional and deep learning-based baselines in NMSE performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The application of diffusion models to channel estimation in RIS-assisted systems is a relatively new area, although it's been explored by other works such as [9], [10], and [11]. The novelty of this paper lies primarily in the specific design choices:

*   **Deterministic Sampling and Step Alignment:**  The core novelty is the combination of deterministic reverse process (addressing the stochasticity inherent in DMs) with the step alignment mechanism. This aims to dynamically adapt the denoising process based on SNR levels. This idea of leveraging a known signal structure for guiding DM reverse process is relatively novel.
*   **Lightweight Network Architecture (BRCNet):**  While CNN-based lightweight architectures are common, the proposed BRCNet seems to be a tailored design for this specific problem, placing the activation function before the convolutional layer in the BRC block for improved performance. However, similar modifications to CNNs are frequently seen.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a relevant challenge: channel estimation in RIS-assisted systems, where accurate CSI is crucial for performance.  The advantages of using diffusion models here are the potential to model complex channel distributions and adapt to varying noise conditions without retraining, a limitation of direct mapping deep learning approaches. The reduced complexity of the BRCNet makes the method more practical for real-world deployment. The performance gains demonstrated compared to other channel estimation methods indicate a significant impact on the channel estimation accuracy.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Formulation:** The paper clearly formulates the channel estimation problem within the DM framework.
*   **Well-Justified Design Choices:** The authors provide strong justifications for their design choices, particularly the step alignment mechanism and the lightweight architecture.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The simulations are comprehensive, comparing against several baselines and exploring the impact of different design parameters.
*   **Practical Considerations:** The focus on reducing network complexity is important for real-world deployment.
*   **Relatively comprehensive comparisons**: The paper compares against relevant benchmarks, including deep learning alternatives.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** The individual components of the proposed method (CNN architectures, denoising reverse process) are not entirely novel. The contribution mainly lies in integrating them in a specific way for this application.
*   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the paper provides justifications, a more rigorous theoretical analysis of the convergence properties of the deterministic sampling strategy would be beneficial.
*   **Generalizability:** It is difficult to ascertain how well the performance of the model will be in more realistic channel models. More complex or hardware-measured channels would improve the credibility.
*   **Scalability:** The paper only considers a relatively small-scale system (8x8 BS array and 4x4 RIS array). It would be beneficial to see how the proposed method scales to larger systems.
*   **Reproducibility**: The parameters selected by the authors for the deep learning models are likely the result of an extensive optimization process. Some guidance for the reader on parameter selection will improve the reproducibility of the work.

**Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more robust and efficient channel estimation techniques for RIS-assisted wireless systems. The lightweight architecture makes the method more practical, and the adaptive step alignment mechanism can improve performance in dynamic environments.

**Rationale for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7** because:

*   The paper addresses a relevant problem with a novel application of DMs.
*   The combination of deterministic sampling, step alignment, and a lightweight architecture offers a practical and effective solution.
*   The experimental results demonstrate clear performance improvements over baselines.

However, the novelty is incremental, and a deeper theoretical analysis and more extensive evaluation are needed to fully assess its long-term impact. The relatively small scale of the simulations also limits the assessment of scalability.
